**Статус: Исправлено.** Пакет Serilog.AspNetCore снят с DM.Domain.Account: обогащение лога именем вызывающего переехало в AuthenticationMiddleware, где PushProperty обернут в using и фрейм снимается. Заодно ушел workaround IIdentitySetter.Refresh - он существовал только чтобы перетолкнуть потерянное свойство: ErrorHandlingMiddleware стоит выше аутентификации, поэтому две его записи о нарушении доступа и необработанной ошибке теперь называют пользователя параметром, а не берут из ambient-контекста

`src/DM.Domain.Account/DM.Domain.Account.csproj` references `Serilog.AspNetCore` — the only domain project to do so — and the sole usage is `Features/Identity/IdentityProvider.cs:3` `using Serilog.Context;` with `:23` `LogContext.PushProperty("User", _identity.User.Username);` inside the `Current` property setter. `PushProperty` returns an `IDisposable` that pops the enricher off the ambient AsyncLocal stack; the return value is discarded, so nothing is ever popped.

Последствие: An ASP.NET-Core-flavoured logging package sits in a domain module for one line of enrichment, which is a middleware concern. The discarded disposable means every assignment to `Current` within a scope pushes another frame that is never removed — harmless in the normal single-assignment request path, but it makes the enricher's behaviour dependent on how many times identity is set, which is not something a domain type should control.

Исправление: Move the enrichment to the existing `AuthenticationMiddleware`/`CorrelationMiddleware` in Web.API (which already owns `ActivityEnricher` in Infrastructure.Core/Logging), and drop the Serilog package from the domain csproj.


---

# Реляционное хранилище и EF Core — D+

The schema contains a production-fatal contradiction (two mutually exclusive FKs on one column, breaking all game/blog invitations), there is zero optimistic concurrency and effectively zero transaction usage across ~70 repositories, the single-InitialCreate policy leaves no upgrade path for any deployed database and the hand-edited migration is never executed by any test — all of which outweighs genuinely sophisticated work in full-text search, partial indexes and check constraints.

## Что сделано хорошо

- Partial-index idiom for soft-delete uniqueness is correct and applied consistently: `HasFilter("\"IsRemoved\" = false")` on the unique (AuthorId, GameId)/(AuthorId, TargetUserId)/(AuthorId, PostId) indexes (DmDbContext.cs:112-163) lets a user re-review after deleting an old one, which a plain UNIQUE would block. This is a subtlety most codebases get wrong.
- PeriodDigestTopic uses two separate partial unique indexes — `(Year, Month) WHERE Month IS NOT NULL` and `(Year) WHERE Month IS NULL` (DmDbContext.cs:200-211) — because NULLs are distinct in a unique index. The comment explicitly names the check-then-insert race and calls the index "the concurrency backstop". That is exactly right reasoning, and it is the only place in the persistence layer where a race is actually closed at the DB level.
- The tsvector design is the best part of the layer: STORED generated columns with `regexp_replace` stripping `[private=…]…[/private]` BEFORE tokenization plus GIN indexes (DmDbContext.cs:231-249). Private text is never in the index, so it cannot be matched or previewed regardless of application bugs — the security invariant is enforced by the storage engine, not by a code path someone can forget. The snippet builder re-applies the same regex server-side (MessageSearchRepository.cs:38-41).
- Polymorphic "typed target" columns are enforced by CHECK constraints rather than by convention: CK_Uploads_TypedTarget ties the Type discriminator to exactly one non-null FK column (DmDbContext.cs:558-562), CK_RoomAccesses_TypedTarget does the same for Character/ReaderUser (DmDbContext.cs:591-594), each with matching partial indexes. This is the correct way to do polymorphism in a relational store.
- `Page()` is declared on `IOrderedQueryable<T>`, not `IQueryable<T>` (QueryableExtensions.cs:18) — the type system structurally forbids paging an unordered query. Small decision, large payoff: nondeterministic pagination is impossible by construction.
- 122 `TagWith(...)` call sites across the repositories. Every query in pg_stat_statements is attributable to a named code path without a profiler — real operational maturity that most projects never invest in.
- List paths deliberately replace per-row correlated subqueries with batched GROUP BY hydration (TopicRepository.cs:118-146, GameRepository.cs:160-290, UserRepository.cs:672-960), and the mapping profiles `Ignore()` the aggregate members with a written rationale (TopicMappingProfile.cs:10-16, 33-41). The N+1 problem was clearly understood and attacked systematically.
- `AsSplitQuery()` is applied precisely where multiple collections would cartesian-explode, with the reason documented and the ordering made total via a GameId tiebreak so the split subqueries select the same page (GameRepository.cs:63-78, BlogRepository.cs:294-309).
- `DateRangeFilters.WhereAtOrBefore` (Shared/Queries/DateRangeFilters.cs) is a single source of truth for inclusive "to" date semantics, correctly handling the whole-day case, the calendar-edge overflow, nullable properties, and providing the matching Mongo variant. `AvatarProjections` (Shared/Users/AvatarProjections.cs) does the same for the avatar projection. Both are EF-translatable, so the SSOT survives into SQL rather than collapsing to in-memory evaluation.
- `UpdateBuilder` (RelationalStorage/UpdateBuilder.cs) attaches a PK-only stub and flags individual properties modified, producing `UPDATE … SET col=@p WHERE id=@id` without a preceding SELECT — a genuinely useful partial-update primitive that most EF codebases fake with load-then-mutate.
- Rating increments use `ExecuteUpdateAsync(u => u.SetProperty(x => x.QuantityRating, x => x.QuantityRating + 1))` (PostRepository.cs:433, 493) — a server-side atomic increment, the correct pattern for a counter under concurrency.

## Подтвержденные находки

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] P-01 — Tokens.EntityId carries two simultaneous FKs (Blogs AND Games) — every game/blog invitation INSERT is impossible

**Статус: Исправлено.** Двойной FK снят, 2 теста на реальной миграции red-green (4763ea2a)

**Доказательство.** Token.cs:77-84 declares two navigations on the same column: `[ForeignKey(nameof(EntityId))] public virtual Game.Game? Game` and `[ForeignKey(nameof(EntityId))] public virtual Blog.Blog? Blog`. EF materialised both into DDL — Migrations/20260516025527_InitialCreate.cs:1023-1033 creates BOTH `FK_Tokens_Blogs_EntityId → Blogs.BlogId` AND `FK_Tokens_Games_EntityId → Games.GameId` on column `Tokens.EntityId`; DmDbContextModelSnapshot.cs:2902-2908 confirms both in the model. Writers: GameInvitationRepository.cs:321-333 (`EntityId = entity.GameId`) and BlogInvitationRepository.cs:53-61 (`EntityId = entity.BlogId`). In PostgreSQL a non-null value must satisfy every FK on the column, so EntityId must simultaneously exist in Blogs and in Games. Note the team already hit this exact class of bug once for comments and patched it by hand (InitialCreate.cs:3043-3048: "NOTE: FK_Comments_Topics_EntityId 

**Последствие.** Every invitation flow — game player, game reader, game assistant, blog assistant — fails at SaveChanges with SQLSTATE 23503 on a real database. These are core product features. The failure is invisible in development because the seed and the test suite never create such a token.

**Исправление.** Drop both relationships from the model (map Game/Blog navigations as `[NotMapped]` like Blog.Comments/Game.Comments already are, per the note in DmDbContext.cs:417-427) and remove the two `table.ForeignKey` blocks from InitialCreate.cs:1023-1033. Then add an integration test that creates a game invitation against the Testcontainer DB. Longer term, replace polymorphic EntityId with typed nullable columns + a CHECK discriminator — the pattern already used correctly for Uploads and RoomAccesses.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Every cited fact checks out; no guard prevents it. Token.cs:77-84 declares both navigations on EntityId with no [NotMapped]; DmDbContextModelSnapshot.cs:2902-2909 keeps both in the model; InitialCreate.cs:1024-1033 emits both DDL constraints and a repo-wide grep for FK_Tokens_(Blogs|Games) matches only that file (never dropped, no raw SQL, no docker PG init script, only one migration). DmDbContext.cs:398-412 configures only Token's User relations and contains no Ignore(). Writers set non-null EntityId: GameInvitationRepository.cs:321-333 and BlogInvitationRepository.cs:53-63; PostgreSQL checks each FK independently, so the insert cannot satisfy both — 23503. Flows are wired via GameInvitationController.cs / BlogInvitationController.cs / InvitationController.cs. Nothing catches it: invitation tests are mock-based unit tests, and DatabaseFixture.cs:71 uses EnsureCreatedAsync (same model, s

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] P-02 — The single-InitialCreate policy has no production upgrade path, and the one migration that exists is hand-edited and never executed by any test

**Статус: Частично.** Миграция исполняется всеми 268 интеграционными тестами и теперь безопасно пересоздается: сид, расширение и выражения-индексы больше не рукописные (7e13b41f, f9c59cfe, c8afd670). Отсутствие пути обновления схемы при появлении боевых данных не менялось - триггер окончания правила записан в DATA_STORAGE.md

**Доказательство.** DATA_STORAGE.md:134-139 and LOCAL_SETUP.md:187-191 prescribe `dotnet ef migrations remove` + `dotnet ef migrations add InitialCreate` for every schema change. That mints a new timestamped migration id, so any database already carrying `20260516025527_InitialCreate` in __EFMigrationsHistory will attempt to re-run the full CREATE TABLE script and abort. `Database.Migrate()` is the deploy mechanism (Startup.cs:406, `DM_MigrateOnStart: true` in docker/docker-compose.yml:427). Worse, the generated file is manually patched afterwards — InitialCreate.cs:3043-3048 documents a deleted `AddForeignKey` block and warns "When the migration is regenerated this block must be removed again" — but that instruction lives only in a code comment, not in either doc that describes the procedure. And the migration is never executed anywhere in CI: DatabaseFixture.cs:71 uses `await context.Database.EnsureCreate

**Последствие.** Once real user data exists, no schema change can be deployed. The only documented recovery is a full dump restore, i.e. a data-loss window on every schema change. Meanwhile the production DDL has zero test coverage, so hand-edits (present) and model/migration drift (P-01 proves it exists) ship undetected.

**Исправление.** Keep the squash-into-InitialCreate convention for pre-launch, but write down the exit criterion and the escape hatch now: from the first production deploy, additive migrations only. Immediately switch DatabaseFixture.cs:71 from `EnsureCreatedAsync()` to `MigrateAsync()` so CI executes the actual production DDL — that single change would have caught P-01.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Every citation holds under inspection, and the failure mode has already been armed in this repo. DATA_STORAGE.md:134-139 and LOCAL_SETUP.md:187-191 do prescribe `migrations remove` + `migrations add InitialCreate`; Startup.cs:404-407 runs `dbContext.Database.Migrate(); Environment.Exit(0);` as a one-shot job wired to docker-compose.yml:418-435 (`DM_MigrateOnStart: true`), and dmapi gates on it via `depends_on: migration: condition: service_completed_successfully` (docker-compose.yml:538-540), so a migration failure is a hard start failure, not a silent one. The id churn is not hypothetical: `git log --follow` shows the file renamed 20260303205706_InitialCreate.cs -> 20260516025527_InitialCreate.cs in HEAD commit 0ed49eff, and pgdata is a persistent named volume (docker-compose.yml:9, :98) — so the next `docker compose up` against any environment stamped with the old id replays the full C

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-03 — No optimistic concurrency anywhere, and immediate ExecuteUpdate is mixed with deferred SaveChanges outside any transaction

**Статус: Исправлено.** PostRepository.Create обернут в транзакцию внутри execution strategy: инкремент QuantityRating и сдвиг активности игры выполнялись немедленно, а вставка поста откладывалась до SaveChanges, поэтому сбой между ними оставлял рейтинг автора и метку активности от несуществующего поста. Попутно найдена и исправлена активная поломка: TopicRepository.Create открывал транзакцию напрямую, а в хосте API включен EnableRetryOnFailure - EF отказывается от пользовательской транзакции при retrying-стратегии, то есть создание тем падало в бою. Тесты этого не ловили, потому что фабрика интеграционных тестов регистрировала DbContext без retry; фабрика приведена к настройкам Startup, после чего четыре теста BoardTopicSummary упали именно с этой ошибкой, и снова стали зелеными после фикса. Токены конкурентности (rowversion) по-прежнему отсутствуют - это отдельный вопрос

**Доказательство.** `grep -rn "IsRowVersion|ConcurrencyCheck|IsConcurrencyToken|xmin|DbUpdateConcurrencyException"` over src/ and test/ returns zero hits. `BeginTransaction` appears exactly once in the entire solution (ActivationRepository.cs:69-72). PostRepository.Create is the archetype: `_dbContext.Posts.Add(dbPost)` (deferred), then `ExecuteUpdateAsync(... QuantityRating + 1)` at line 433 (executes and auto-commits immediately), then another `ExecuteUpdateAsync` on Games at 441, then `SaveChangesAsync()` at 446. There is no ambient transaction. `EnableRetryOnFailure(maxRetryCount: 3)` is configured (Startup.cs:129-132), which makes each statement independently retryable.

**Последствие.** If SaveChanges at PostRepository.cs:446 fails or the request is cancelled, the author's QuantityRating has already been permanently incremented for a post that does not exist, and Game.LastPostCreatedUtc/InactivityWarningUtc are already mutated. QuantityRating drives the user rating and the stored computed column `IsNewbie` (DmDbContext.cs:437-439), so the drift is user-visible and unrecoverable. ExecuteUpdate also bypasses the change tracker, leaving any User already tracked in that scope stale. Combined with the absence of rowversion, every read-modify-write counter in the codebase silently loses concurrent updates.

**Исправление.** Wrap Create in `Database.CreateExecutionStrategy().ExecuteAsync(...)` + an explicit transaction, exactly as ActivationRepository.cs:68-72 already demonstrates — the correct pattern exists in the codebase and is used once. Add a `[Timestamp] uint xmin` concurrency token to the entities that carry mutable denormalized counters (User, Board, Topic, Blog, Game) and handle DbUpdateConcurrencyException with a retry.

**Проверка оппонентом.** The mechanical core survives verification, but half the supporting reasoning is false and "critical" is inflated by two notches.

WHAT HOLDS (verified line-by-line):
- PostRepository.cs:428 `_dbContext.Posts.Add(dbPost)` (deferred) → :431-433 `ExecuteUpdateAsync(QuantityRating + 1)` (executes + autocommits immediately) → :436 `Rooms.FindAsync` → :439-443 `ExecuteUpdateAsync` on Games → :446 `SaveChangesAsync()`. No ambient transaction. Exactly as described.
- `grep` for IsRowVersion|ConcurrencyCheck|IsConcurrencyToken|DbUpdateConcurrencyException|[Timestamp]|xmin over src/ and test/ returns zero hits. Confirmed.
- `BeginTransaction` appears exactly once: ActivationRepository.cs:72, inside the correct `CreateExecutionStrategy().ExecuteAsync` wrapper (lines 69-93). Confirmed.
- Startup.cs:129-132 `EnableRetryOnFailure(maxRetryCount: 3, ...)` + CommandTimeout(30). Confirmed.
- DmDbContext.c

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-04 — Index coverage does not match the query set — the hottest predicates have no index, and a code comment claims an index that does not exist

**Статус: Исправлено.** Добавлен составной индекс (EntityType, EntityId) на Likes - у таблицы не было ни одного индекса под реальные предикаты, а читается она либо как "лайки этой сущности", либо как "лайки этого вида" с join по EntityId, поэтому EntityType ведущий: при обратном порядке join-образные чтения (счетчики профиля, статистика сообщества) индекс не взяли бы вовсе. Уточнение к находке: по Topics описание неверно - там четыре индекса, включая составной уникальный (BoardId, TopicNumber), чья ведущая колонка играет роль отдельного BoardId; а предложенные индексы по Games не окупились бы, потому что листинг ведет дизъюнкция из пяти веток и сортировка по COALESCE, которые b-tree не возьмет

**Доказательство.** Extracting all 173 CreateIndex calls from InitialCreate.cs against the actual queries: (a) Likes has only IX_Likes_UserId and IX_Likes_DeletedByUserId, yet every like aggregate filters `(EntityType, EntityId)` — TopicRepository.cs:136-145, UserRepository.cs:773-803, and a correlated per-row subquery in TopicCommentRepository.cs:115-123. TopicRepository.cs:120-125 literally claims "we trade 2N subqueries for 2 indexed lookups" — the Likes lookup is a full sequential scan. (b) Subscriptions has only IX_Subscriptions_SubscriberId, but every read filters `(TargetType, TargetId)` — UserRepository.cs:847-849, GameRepository.cs:258-260, BlogRepository.cs:922. (c) Games has only DeletedByUserId/MasterId/MentorId — nothing on Status, PremoderationStatus, IsRecruitmentOpen or LastPostCreatedUtc, all of which drive the main /games listing (GameRepository.cs:322-378). (d) Topics has only BoardId — n

**Последствие.** The forum index, the games list and every profile page issue sequential scans over the fastest-growing tables. Likes and Subscriptions grow without bound, so the degradation is superlinear and will present as a sudden cliff rather than a gradual slowdown.

**Исправление.** Add composite indexes on Likes(EntityType, EntityId) WHERE "IsRemoved" = false, Subscriptions(TargetType, TargetId), Games(Status, PremoderationStatus) and Games(LastPostCreatedUtc DESC), Topics(BoardId, CreatedUtc DESC), OutboxEvents(Id) WHERE "IsProcessed" = false. Then run the listing endpoints under EXPLAIN ANALYZE against seeded volume — the TagWith labels already in place make this trivial.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Core evidence survives verification, but three of five sub-claims collapse and the impact is overstated, so severity drops to medium.

WHAT IS REAL (verified line-by-line):
1. Likes(EntityType, EntityId) is genuinely unindexed. InitialCreate.cs:2208-2215 creates exactly two Likes indexes (IX_Likes_DeletedByUserId, IX_Likes_UserId); there is no [Index] attribute on Like.cs and no HasIndex for Like in DmDbContext.cs. Meanwhile TopicRepository.cs:136-144 filters !IsRemoved && EntityType == Topic && topicIds.Contains(EntityId) on every forum listing page, and TopicCommentRepository.cs:116-123 puts a correlated Likes.Count(...) inside OrderBy, which PG evaluates per candidate row across the whole filtered set before paging (the only genuinely quadratic path, and only when sortBy=likes). Likes is actively written (LikeRepository.cs:23, ModerationApiService.cs:1591/1882), so the table is not de

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-05 — IX_Users_Username_Lower / IX_Users_Email_Lower are plain b-trees on the raw column — the names are false and every login seq-scans Users

**Статус: Исправлено.** Индексы стали выражением lower(...), план сменился с Seq Scan на Index Scan (fb8d3cf6)

**Доказательство.** DmDbContext.cs:470-482 creates these under the comment "Case-insensitive username and email indexes for efficient lookups in PostgreSQL", but `HasIndex(u => u.Username).HasDatabaseName("IX_Users_Username_Lower")` produces an ordinary index on the raw column — InitialCreate.cs:2814-2823 confirms `column: "Email"` / `column: "Username"` with no expression. Every lookup then uses a form PostgreSQL cannot match to that index: `u.Email.ToLower() == email.ToLower()` (AuthenticationRepository.cs:41 — the login path), `u.Username.ToLower() == username.ToLower()` (EmailChangeRepository.cs:32, PasswordChangeRepository.cs:29, RegistrationRepository.cs:36-37, UsernameChangeRepository.cs:212, UserRepository.cs:183/217, BlogRepository.cs:319), and `EF.Functions.ILike(u.Email, email)` (EmailLookupRepository.cs:24/39, RegistrationRepository.cs:26, UserRepository.cs:267).

**Последствие.** Every authentication, every registration availability check and every profile-by-username fetch is a sequential scan of Users. It also means the UNIQUE constraints are case-SENSITIVE at the DB level, so 'Ivan' and 'ivan' can both be inserted — the case-insensitivity promised by USERNAME_POLICY.md is enforced only by an application-level `AnyAsync` (RegistrationRepository.cs:36), which is a check-then-act race.

**Исправление.** Either make the columns `citext` (one type change, everything else keeps working and the unique constraints become genuinely case-insensitive), or add expression indexes `ON "Users" (lower("Username"))` / `(lower("Email"))` and standardise every call site on `lower() =` — ILIKE without a wildcard will still not use a lower() index.

**Проверка оппонентом.** I tried to break this and could not — every cite is literally accurate.

VERIFIED FACTS
1. D:\Code Projects\dm3\src\DM.Infrastructure.Persistence\DmDbContext.cs:470-482 — under the comment "Case-insensitive username and email indexes for efficient lookups in PostgreSQL" it emits `HasIndex(u => u.Username).HasDatabaseName("IX_Users_Username_Lower").IsUnique()` and the same for Email. There is no expression, no `HasComputedColumnSql`, no filter.
2. D:\Code Projects\dm3\src\DM.Infrastructure.Persistence\Migrations\20260516025527_InitialCreate.cs:2813-2823 emits `name: "IX_Users_Email_Lower", column: "Email", unique: true` and `name: "IX_Users_Username_Lower", column: "Username", unique: true` — plain b-trees on the raw columns. The names are false.
3. No escape hatch exists. I grepped the whole solution for `citext`, `HasCollation`, `UseCollation`, `HasPostgresExtension`, non-deterministic 

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-06 — Keyset pagination tie-break is logically wrong and the ORDER BY has no tiebreak at all, despite an index built specifically for one

**Статус: Исправлено.** Тотальный порядок + sargable-предикат (d11d99e7); ревью нашло off-by-one окна и ложную мотивировку в Delete — закрыто в f9b077bc

**Доказательство.** DmDbContext.cs:251-254 creates IX_Messages_ChatId_CreatedUtc_MessageId with the comment "filter by ChatId, order by CreatedUtc, tie-break by MessageId". No query does that. MessageRepository.cs:94-95 pages backwards with `Where(m => m.CreatedUtc < ts || (m.CreatedUtc == ts && m.MessageId != messageId)).OrderByDescending(m => m.CreatedUtc)` — `!=` is not an ordering, so every other message sharing the timestamp qualifies regardless of which side of the cursor it sits on, and MessageId never appears in the ORDER BY. Identical predicate at lines 105, 113, 124. MessageSearchRepository.cs repeats it three times (lines 100, 131, 162) and then orders the UNION by `OrderByDescending(h => h.CreatedUtc)` alone (line 185). GetAround is worse: lines 145-161 use strict `<` and `>` against the reference timestamp, so any other message with the exact same CreatedUtc is dropped from the window entirely.

**Последствие.** Chat scrollback and the message/post search return duplicated and skipped rows whenever timestamps collide — which is routine in a busy global chat and guaranteed for bulk-imported legacy data from l.dm.am. The purpose-built composite index is dead weight because no query can use it for ordering.

**Исправление.** Make the sort total everywhere: `.OrderByDescending(m => m.CreatedUtc).ThenByDescending(m => m.MessageId)` and change the predicate to `m.CreatedUtc < ts || (m.CreatedUtc == ts && m.MessageId < messageId)`. Mirror it in MessageSearchRepository (including the post branch at line 162) and fix GetAround to use `<=`/`>=` with the MessageId tiebreak.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Code evidence verified line-for-line and could not be refuted: MessageRepository.cs:94-95/105/113/124 use `m.MessageId != messageId` as a tie-break (not an ordering) with `OrderByDescending(m => m.CreatedUtc)` and no ThenBy; GetAround (MessageRepository.cs:145,157) uses strict `<`/`>` so a message sharing the reference CreatedUtc is dropped from both halves; MessageSearchRepository.cs:99-100/130-131/161-162 repeat the predicate and line 185 orders the concatenated branches by CreatedUtc alone; DmDbContext.cs:251-254 does document "tie-break by MessageId" for an index no query tiebreaks on. No guard exists — MessageFactory.cs:26 -> DateTimeProvider.cs:10 (DateTimeOffset.UtcNow), no uniqueness on the column, and CursorService.cs:28 round-trips the timestamp exactly through System.Text.Json so the `==` branch is live code, not dead. HOWEVER the severity is inflated because three impact clai

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] P-07 — Board denormalized last-comment/count columns are written only by the dev seeder; topic delete and topic move never repair them

**Статус: Исправлено.** Вторая половина закрыта по решению владельца: семь денормализованных колонок про комментарии (CommentsCount и шесть LastComment*) удалены, значение считается на чтении. У них не было ни одного писателя в рантайме - их заполнял только демо-сидер, поэтому в бою форум показывал 0 комментариев у всех одиннадцати досок навсегда, а "последняя активность" не могла назвать комментарий никогда. Половина про темы оставлена денормализованной: ее пересчет уже подключен к созданию, удалению и переносу, то есть правилу "счетчик обновляется при изменении" она удовлетворяет. Попутно снят часовой кеш списка досок: он не инвалидировался ничем, а per-user счетчики непрочитанного писались прямо в разделяемые объекты кеша, то есть числа одного читателя могли всплыть у другого. Добавлен интеграционный тест на то, что комментарий виден в счетчике сразу

**Доказательство.** Board carries 14 denormalized columns (Board.cs:52-113: TopicsCount, CommentsCount, LastCommentId/TopicId/TopicTitle/TopicNumber/AuthorId/Utc, LastTopicId/Number/Title/AuthorId/CreatedUtc) plus 4 FKs and 4 indexes. Grepping every writer: the only production writer is TopicRepository.cs:342-352 (Create sets LastTopic* and `board.TopicsCount++`). The LastComment* group and CommentsCount are written exclusively by the development seeder — ModerationApiService.cs:2305, 2332-2336. TopicCommentRepository.Create/Delete (lines 156-166, 232-248) update Topic but never touch Board. TopicRepository.Delete (lines 404-412) only sets IsRemoved — no TopicsCount decrement, no LastTopic* recompute. TopicRepository.Update (lines 362-395) can change the title and even reassign `topic.BoardId` with no denormalization update on either board.

**Последствие.** The forum index permanently displays a "last comment" frozen at whatever the seed produced. After the first topic deletion the board's last-topic link points at a soft-deleted topic (and TopicsCount is inflated forever); after a topic move, both boards show wrong counts and the source board still advertises the moved topic. `board.TopicsCount++` is also an in-memory read-modify-write, so concurrent creates lose increments.

**Исправление.** Delete the LastComment*/CommentsCount columns outright (nothing reads a correct value today) or maintain them from the comment repositories in the same SaveChanges. Recompute LastTopic* on delete/move, and replace `board.TopicsCount++` with `ExecuteUpdateAsync(b => b.SetProperty(x => x.TopicsCount, x => x.TopicsCount + 1))`.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Every load-bearing claim verified against the source; all four refutation angles failed.

WRITERS (grep of every reference to the denorm fields, Migrations excluded, yields exactly these): TopicRepository.cs:343-352 sets LastTopicId/Number/Title/AuthorId/CreatedUtc and does board.TopicsCount++; PeriodDigestService.cs:215-223 ExecuteUpdateAsync recomputes LastTopic* for the News board only, after backdating a digest topic; ModerationApiService.cs:2304-2305 and 2332-2337 (dev seeder) is the ONLY writer of LastCommentId/TopicId/TopicTitle/TopicNumber/AuthorId/Utc and of CommentsCount. Nothing else writes them. No DB trigger, no computed column (migration has only pg_trgm + three tsvector/bool computed columns), no ExecuteSqlRaw against Boards.

NON-WRITERS confirmed as claimed: TopicCommentRepository.Create (156-166) and Delete (232-249) update Topic.CommentCount/Topic.LastCommentId and nev

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-08 — TopicNumber is assigned by MAX+1 with no unique constraint — duplicate numbers break topic URLs

**Статус: Исправлено.** Уникальный индекс (BoardId, TopicNumber) + FOR UPDATE + IgnoreQueryFilters, 3 теста red-green (7d281537)

**Доказательство.** TopicRepository.cs:318-323 computes `maxTopicNumber = await ... .Where(t => t.BoardId == boardId).Select(t => (int?)t.TopicNumber).MaxAsync() ?? 0` and then `topicNumber = maxTopicNumber + 1`, outside any transaction or lock. The migration creates no unique index on (BoardId, TopicNumber) — Topics has only IX_Topics_AuthorId, IX_Topics_BoardId, IX_Topics_DeletedByUserId, IX_Topics_LastCommentId. That number is the URL key: GetByBoardAndNumber(boardId, topicNumber) at TopicRepository.cs:246-259 and ExistsByBoardAndNumber at 240-244 resolve topics by it. Contrast with Chat, which does this correctly via a DB `[DatabaseGenerated(DatabaseGeneratedOption.Identity)] SerialNumber` (Chat.cs:24-27).

**Последствие.** Two topics created in the same board within the same instant get the same number. `FirstOrDefaultAsync` then resolves the URL to an arbitrary one of them, permanently making one topic unreachable by its canonical link. There is no constraint to even detect that it happened.

**Исправление.** Add a unique index on (BoardId, TopicNumber) as a backstop, and switch allocation to a DB-side sequence or `INSERT … SELECT coalesce(max(...),0)+1` in a single statement — the Identity pattern already used by Chat.SerialNumber is the model to copy.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Every cited fact verified. TopicRepository.cs:318-323 does compute `MaxAsync(ct) ?? 0` + 1 and then Add/SaveChangesAsync at :340-354 with no transaction, lock, or retry (the solution's only BeginTransaction is ActivationRepository.cs:69-72). The migration creates only IX_Topics_AuthorId/BoardId/DeletedByUserId/LastCommentId (InitialCreate.cs:2613-2628) and the table has just PK_Topics on TopicId (:1050-1084); DmDbContext.cs configures no HasIndex for Topics, and no raw SQL adds one — so there is genuinely no unique constraint on (BoardId, TopicNumber). TopicNumber really is the canonical URL key (URL_STRUCTURE.md:66-67,117,176; TopicController.cs:180; TopicsList.vue:174, ProfileTopicsList.vue:128, TopicView.vue:71), and the impact is not softened by an id fallback: TopicRedirect.vue:21-25 resolves a topic by GUID only to router.replace into {alias, num}, and the SPA has no id-based topic

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-09 — Subscriber lists are loaded unbounded from the DB and truncated to 20 in memory

**Статус: Частично.** Индекс (TargetType, TargetId) снял seq scan (da726490); полная выборка подписчиков с Take(20) в памяти осталась — в двух из трех мест тот же предикат уже выбирается целиком выше, нужен один проход вместо двух

**Доказательство.** UserRepository.cs:847-879 selects every Subscriptions row for the page's users (`Where(s => s.TargetType == User && userIds.Contains(s.TargetId))`), materialises it with ToListAsync, and only then applies `.Take(20)` three times in LINQ-to-Objects (lines 866, 872). GameRepository.cs:258-268 and BlogRepository.cs:922-931 repeat the same shape with the same 20-row cap. The comment at UserRepository.cs:846 ("Limit retained at 20 per user") describes an intent the SQL does not implement.

**Последствие.** A profile page for a popular user, or a games list page containing a popular game, pulls every subscriber row across the wire to display 20 names. On a user-list page of 20 users this multiplies. Combined with the missing (TargetType, TargetId) index from P-04, each of these is a full scan of Subscriptions.

**Исправление.** Push the cap into SQL — a lateral/window query (`ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY TargetId ORDER BY ...) <= 20`) for the names, and a separate `GROUP BY TargetId COUNT(*)` for the total. The batched-GROUP-BY counts elsewhere in the same methods already show the right shape.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Verified against the real code; every refutation ground failed. (1) The quotes are exact: UserRepository.cs:847-856 materialises every Subscriptions row for the page's users with no SQL Take, then applies .Take(20) in LINQ-to-Objects at lines 866 and 872, and derives SubscribersCount at 858-860 from that same materialised list; GameRepository.cs:258-267 and BlogRepository.cs:922-931 repeat it verbatim. (2) No guard exists anywhere: no SQL cap, no config limit, and critically no supporting index — Migrations/20260516025527_InitialCreate.cs:2533-2535 shows the ONLY index on Subscriptions is IX_Subscriptions_SubscriberId, so filtering on (TargetType, TargetId) is a scan, exactly as the finding claims. (3) It is not deliberate-and-documented: the comment at UserRepository.cs:846 and the one at GameRepository.cs:257 document a DTO field budget, not a persistence strategy. (4) It is trivially 

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-10 — The outbox pattern is documented as a delivery guarantee but has no writer; the processor polls an always-empty, un-indexed table with no claim

**Статус: Частично.** Каркас outbox удален из кода и миграции; в живой базе таблица-сирота осталась, снимается ресетом (1876e028)

**Доказательство.** DATA_STORAGE.md:124-126 states the outbox "guarantees the event will not be lost even on failure". The table, entity and DbSet exist (OutboxEvent.cs, DmDbContext.cs:723) and OutboxProcessor is registered as a hosted service (Startup.cs:372). But grepping the whole solution for OutboxEvent producers returns only the DbSet declaration and the migration — nothing ever inserts a row. Domain code instead fires directly at the broker after SaveChanges, e.g. TopicCommentService.cs:145 `await _invokedEventProducer.SendAsync(EventType.ChangedTopicComment, ...)`. The processor's query (OutboxProcessor.cs:70-74) has no supporting index and no `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` / claim column, while DEPLOYMENT.md:145 recommends `deploy.replicas: 3`.

**Последствие.** The documented at-least-once guarantee does not exist: an event fired after a successful commit is lost if the broker is unreachable. When the outbox is eventually wired up, three replicas will each read and send the same batch — duplicate events — and the poll will seq-scan a table that is never pruned.

**Исправление.** Either wire the outbox up properly (insert the OutboxEvent inside the same SaveChanges as the state change, claim rows with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, add a partial index on unprocessed rows, and add a pruning job), or delete the table, entity, processor and the DATA_STORAGE.md claim. A documented guarantee that is not implemented is worse than no guarantee.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Core evidence verified and reproducible: DATA_STORAGE.md:124 states the outbox guarantees no event loss, and an exhaustive repo-wide grep for "Outbox" (excluding bin/obj/node_modules/.git) returns only 13 files — two docs, OutboxProcessor.cs, OutboxConfiguration.cs, OutboxEvent.cs, DmDbContext.cs:723, three migration files, MessageQueuingModule.cs, two Startups and one test — with NO code path anywhere inserting an OutboxEvent. Migration 20260516025527_InitialCreate.cs:97-115 creates the table with PK_OutboxEvents only (no IX_OutboxEvents), and OutboxProcessor.cs:70-74 has no claim column / FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED. Domain code fires directly at the broker (src/DM.Domain.Forum/Features/Comments/TopicCommentService.cs:145 — the finding's path was wrong), and InvokedEventProducer.cs:23-28 propagates broker exceptions, so a RabbitMQ outage both drops the event and returns 500 after the DB com

## Средние и низкие

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-11 — A single-user profile read costs 22 sequential database round-trips

**Статус: Открыто.** 

GetUserAsync/GetUserDetailsAsync (UserRepository.cs:179-260) call `PopulatePostReviewCounts(new[] { user })` on a one-element array. That method (lines 672-960) then issues 22 separate awaited queries — post reviews given/received, endorsements given/received, topics, comments, global chat messages, bans, drops, publications, four separate like joins, games by master/assistant/playing, blogs owner/assistant, subscribers, username history. The batching is correct for a 20-row list page but there is no single-entity path.

Последствие: Every profile view, and every internal GetUserAsync, serialises 22 network round-trips. At 1ms RTT that is 22ms of pure latency floor before any of the scans from P-04 are counted.

Исправление: Split the hydration: keep the batched version for lists, and for the single-user case either issue one query with correlated scalar subqueries (one round-trip, PostgreSQL handles this well) or make the achievement-metric counters materialised columns refreshed by the existing event workers.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-12 — 19 Include() chains are silently discarded because a projection follows

**Статус: Исправлено.** Найдено больше, чем в находке: 41 мертвая цепочка вместо 19. Удалены только те, где проекция (ProjectTo/Select/AnyAsync) доказуемо достигается в том же выражении; 22 живых Include с материализацией сущностей не тронуты. SQL не меняется - EF эти Include и так отбрасывал

EF Core ignores Include when the query ends in a projection (it emits an IncludeIgnoredWarning). 19 such sites exist in the persistence project, e.g. UserRepository.cs:92-93 `.Include(u => u.AvatarUpload).Include(u => u.UsernameHistories)` immediately before `.ProjectTo<GeneralUser>()` at line 171, and again at lines 182, 199, 216, 241, 266, 291, 317.

Последствие: Dead code that reads as if it were load-bearing. A maintainer will assume the avatar is eager-loaded and reason incorrectly about the SQL; the risk is that someone "fixes" a null navigation by adding more Includes instead of fixing the mapping profile.

Исправление: Delete every Include that is followed by ProjectTo, and turn IncludeIgnoredWarning into a thrown exception via `ConfigureWarnings(w => w.Throw(CoreEventId.IncludeIgnoredWarning))` so it cannot come back.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-13 — Global soft-delete filter coexists with 258 hand-written !IsRemoved predicates, and one entity with an IsRemoved column is not covered at all

**Статус: Исправлено.** Мертвая колонка PostPendency.IsRemoved удалена вместе с пятью предикатами по ней. Сущность не реализовывала интерфейс мягкого удаления, поэтому глобального фильтра не получала, репозиторий удаляет жестко, а true в этот флаг не писал никто во всем решении - схема имела форму мягкого удаления при жестком поведении. Вторая половина находки (258 ручных !IsRemoved при живом глобальном фильтре) не трогалась: это стилистическая избыточность, снимать ее массовой правкой рискованнее, чем оставить

DmDbContext.cs:597-607 installs a global query filter for every `IRemovable` type. Yet the repositories contain 258 explicit `!x.IsRemoved` predicates (`grep -c` over src/DM.Infrastructure.Persistence), e.g. UserRepository.cs:544, TopicCommentRepository.cs:42/54. There are zero `IgnoreQueryFilters()` calls anywhere, so no code ever needs the escape hatch. Separately, PostPendency declares `public bool IsRemoved` (PostPendency.cs:57) but implements no interface, so it gets no global filter — and PostPendencyRepository.Delete (lines 61-69) does a hard `Remove()`, leaving the column permanently dead.

Последствие: Two mechanisms for one invariant. A reader cannot tell whether a missing `!IsRemoved` at a given call site is a bug or is covered by the filter, and the redundancy makes the filter easy to remove "because everything filters explicitly anyway". PostPendency has soft-delete-shaped schema with hard-delete behaviour.

Исправление: Pick one. Since the global filter is universal and never bypassed, delete the manual predicates and rely on it. Make PostPendency implement IRemovable (and soft-delete it) or drop the IsRemoved column.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-14 — Topic.CommentCount is a write-only column fed from two different sources on create versus delete

**Статус: Исправлено.** Topic.CommentCount удалена. Она писалась из двух разных источников (живой COUNT при создании, сохраненная колонка при удалении), читалась только чтобы вычислить собственное следующее значение, и была явно Ignore-нута в профиле маппинга, то есть на экран не попадала никогда - все отображения считают живьем. Последний ее потребитель, счетчик комментариев доски в сидере, исчез вместе с самим счетчиком. Удалены и поля-переносчики NewCommentCount/TopicCommentCount

On create, TopicCommentService.cs:86 computes `NewCommentCount = topic.TotalCommentsCount + 1`, where TotalCommentsCount came from a live `COUNT(*)` (TopicRepository.FillCounts, lines 290-306); the repository stores it at TopicCommentRepository.cs:162. On delete, TopicCommentService.cs:170 computes `Math.Max(0, comment.TopicCommentCount - 1)` where TopicCommentCount was read from the stored column (TopicCommentRepository.cs:207-212), stored back at line 244. Meanwhile every display path recomputes the count live and the mapping profile explicitly Ignores it (TopicMappingProfile.cs:33-35) — so Topic.CommentCount is never rendered.

Последствие: A denormalized column that costs a write on every comment operation, is read only to compute its own next value, mixes two sources of truth, and is guaranteed to drift under concurrency (read-modify-write, no transaction, no rowversion). Pure liability.

Исправление: Drop the column and the NewCommentCount plumbing through the service/DTO layer, since the live GROUP BY already backs every display path. If it is later needed for performance, make it a single atomic `ExecuteUpdate(x => x.CommentCount + 1)` in the same transaction as the comment insert.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-15 — AsSplitQuery combined with OFFSET pagination can return an inconsistent page under concurrent writes

**Статус: Принято как исключение.** Проверено: из семи названных мест OFFSET есть только у двух (GameRepository.GetGames, BlogRepository.GetBlogs), у остальных пагинации нет вообще. Риск реален - подзапросы коллекций это отдельные операторы под READ COMMITTED, общего снимка нет. Оставлено осознанно, обоснование записано в коде рядом с AsSplitQuery: окно в миллисекунды против единиц записей в сутки, ущерб - одна чужая коллекция на одной отрисовке, а единственное настоящее лечение это keyset-пагинация, меняющая контракт API

GameRepository.cs:63-78 pages with `.Page(pagingData)` (Skip/Take, QueryableExtensions.cs:23) and then `.AsSplitQuery()`; same combination at BlogRepository.cs:106, 280, 294-309, 463, 551, 575 and GameRepository.cs:741-1014. The comment at GameRepository.cs:64-66 addresses only ordering determinism ("the GameId tiebreaker makes the ordering unique"), which is necessary but not sufficient — each collection subquery re-executes its own OFFSET/FETCH in a separate statement with no snapshot shared between them.

Последствие: If a game is created or soft-deleted between the root query and a collection subquery, the OFFSET window shifts and collections are attached to the wrong parents or dropped. On a games list this shows as a game rendering with another game's tags or assistants.

Исправление: Run the split-query read paths inside a REPEATABLE READ transaction, or split manually: fetch the page of ids first, then load collections with `WHERE GameId IN (@ids)` — which removes the OFFSET from the subqueries entirely.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-16 — All model configuration lives in one 1021-line DbContext, half of it behind an isPostgres branch that can never be false

**Статус: Опровергнуто.** Ключевое утверждение находки ложно: DmDbContext создается на InMemory в четырех фикстурах (BlogRepositoryShould, PostRepositoryShould, RoomRepositoryShould, UploadOrphanCleanupShould), то есть ветка isPostgres регулярно берется по else, и снятие ее сломало бы сборку модели на NpgsqlTsVector. Масштаб тоже завышен: 20 обращений и около 94 строк, это 3.6% файла, а не половина. Реальная часть - размер файла (2596 строк конфигурации в одном методе); вынос в IEntityTypeConfiguration отдельной задачей

DmDbContext.cs holds every index, relationship, check constraint, computed column and seed inline — there is not a single IEntityTypeConfiguration<T> in the project. Roughly 20 configuration blocks are guarded by `if (isPostgres)` derived from `Database.IsNpgsql()` at line 47, e.g. the partial index filters (54-58, 63-67, 75-79), the UsernameChangeRequest unique pending index (454-460), the username/email indexes (471-482), the IsNewbie computed column (435-440) and the whole tsvector section (231-249). Every provider registration in the solution is Npgsql — Startup.cs:126, Workers.NotificationDispatcher/Startup.cs:66, Workers.SearchIndexer/Startup.cs:59, Design/DmDbContextFactory.cs:16 — and no test uses SQLite or InMemory.

Последствие: The branch is dead but not harmless: it encodes that these invariants (including the "one pending username-change request per user" uniqueness) are optional, so on any non-Npgsql provider they silently vanish rather than failing loudly. Combined with the file's size, entity configuration is hard to locate and hard to review — which is how P-01 slipped through.

Исправление: Split the configuration into per-entity IEntityTypeConfiguration classes next to their entities and register with ApplyConfigurationsFromAssembly. Delete the isPostgres guards; the project targets PostgreSQL and the migration is PostgreSQL-specific already.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] P-17 — Tracking discipline is ad hoc — AsNoTracking in 6 files out of ~70, no global default, and full entities materialised on read paths

**Статус: Исправлено.** Проекция вместо материализации сущности: полные строки User вместе с Salt и PasswordHash больше не попадают в память на каждого активного персонажа страницы. Ловушка, на которую указал проверяющий, учтена - фильтр c.Author != null перенесен ДО проекции, иначе EF не перевел бы его; QuantityRating включен в набор полей, без него IsNewbie молча стал бы true у всех игроков. Уточнение к находке: пустые аватары игроков видимого эффекта не имеют - UserRef на границе API вообще не несет картинки, и аватар игрока не рендерится ни на одной поверхности

All 27 AsNoTracking calls live in just 6 files (DeactivationRepository, AchievementRepository, AwardRepository, TopicRepository, PostRepository, and their comments cite PERFORMANCE.md). No `QueryTrackingBehavior.NoTracking` default is configured in Startup.cs:124-135. Most reads are saved by ProjectTo returning non-entity DTOs, but the exceptions are the expensive ones: GameRepository.cs:180-185 selects `new { c.GameId, c.Author }` — materialising and tracking full User entities (including Salt and PasswordHash) for every active character on the page; GameRepository.cs:205-213 does `.Include(t => t.User).ToListAsync()` on tokens.

Последствие: Change-tracker bloat proportional to page size on the hottest list endpoint, plus password hashes and salts pulled into application memory for a read that only needs a username and avatar. Because these reads share a scope with subsequent writes, an accidental mutation would be persisted.

Исправление: Set NoTracking as the context default and opt into tracking explicitly in the write paths (they all use Find/Attach, which tracks regardless). Replace the `c.Author` selection with an explicit projection of the three fields GeneralUser actually needs.

### [НИЗКАЯ] P-18 — The API layer writes to DmDbContext directly, and one 5000-line API service is the sole writer of several schema columns

**Статус: Открыто.** 

Ten files under src/DM.Web.API reference DmDbContext directly, bypassing the repository layer entirely: ModerationApiService.cs, TopicApiService.cs, UploadApiService.cs, CommunityStatsApiService.cs, NotificationApiService.cs and five HostedServices. ModerationApiService.cs alone carries 20 SaveChangesAsync calls and is the only writer of Board.LastCommentId/LastCommentTopicId/LastCommentUtc/CommentsCount (lines 2305, 2326-2336) — see P-07.

Последствие: The persistence boundary that the rest of the solution maintains is not actually a boundary. Schema semantics (which columns exist and who maintains them) can only be discovered by reading the API project, which is why the Board denormalization gap is invisible from the persistence layer.

Исправление: Move the seeding logic behind an explicit ISeedRepository in the persistence project so the API project depends only on repository interfaces, and so the set of writers per column is discoverable in one place.


---

# Полиглотное хранение (PG / Mongo / OpenSearch / MinIO) — D+

The PG/Mongo rule is documented and the one place it is followed rigorously (Postgres tsvector for private content) is genuinely excellent engineering — but three of the four stores have consistency machinery that does not work: the Outbox table has zero writers, the OpenSearch authorization filter is a structural no-op, the MinIO garbage collector can never match a single row, half the Mongo indexes point at fields that do not exist, and cross-store references (Games.AttributeSchemaId → Mongo) are hard-deleted with no compensation.

## Что сделано хорошо

- Postgres tsvector search is the best-engineered thing in this dimension, and it is the part that proves the extra stores are unnecessary. DmDbContext.cs:240-248 puts the [private] strip *inside* the generated STORED column expression, so private BBCode is never tokenized and cannot be matched or previewed even by a bug downstream — that is a defense-in-depth choice, not a filter. MessageSearchRepository.cs:29-41 keeps the strip regex byte-identical to the column expression with a comment explaining why [mod] is deliberately NOT stripped. Access is re-derived from the caller's identity on every keyset page (MessageSearchRepository.cs:99-118) rather than denormalized. DATA_STORAGE.md:128 states the rule *and its reason* ("доступ меняется динамически, а индекс должен быть live-consistent"), and the code obeys it.
- Upload write ordering is correct and the reasoning is written down. UploadApiService.cs:276-321 does S3 PUT → DB insert → compensating S3 delete on DB failure, so the system never produces a DB row pointing at a missing object (only the harmless inverse). RollbackS3PutsAsync (426-443) swallows rollback errors deliberately and says so. UploadGarbageCollector.cs:71-74 explicitly documents the opposite ordering for deletes (DB soft-delete first, then S3) with the correct justification. The Idempotency-Key cache (UploadApiService.cs:161-176) is scoped per user, closing the cross-user replay hole.
- Upload target modeling refuses the easy polymorphic shortcut. DmDbContext.cs:526-563 uses three typed nullable FK columns (TargetUserId/TargetCharacterId/TargetPostId) with real FK constraints, partial indexes, and a CHECK constraint tying the non-null column to the Type discriminator — instead of the (TargetType, TargetId) pair used elsewhere in the codebase. Real referential integrity exactly where orphan rows would be expensive.
- Three of the nine Mongo collections are genuinely document-shaped and would be awkward relationally: DiceRoll with its nested RollResult[] (Entities/Game/Posts/DiceRoll.cs:72), AttributeSchema with per-type polymorphic Constraints (String/Number/List/BbCode subtypes, AttributeSchemaRepository.cs:204-219), and Poll with embedded options each carrying its own voter array (Entities/Forum/Poll.cs:46,78). These are defensible choices, not cargo cult.
- The UnreadCounters access path is properly engineered: the two-stage $group aggregation (UnreadCountersRepository.cs:100-121) does the min-then-sum rollup server-side rather than pulling documents into .NET, and it is the one collection whose four declared indexes in docker/mongo-init.js:17-41 actually match the field names and predicates the repository issues.
- DATA_STORAGE.md is short, non-duplicative, and contains an actual decision procedure ("Как понять, куда положить Entity", lines 58-74) keyed off where the repository interface lives — a mechanical rule an engineer can apply without judgment calls. Most storage docs are prose; this one is a lookup table.

## Подтвержденные находки

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] PG-01 — OpenSearch authorization filter is structurally a no-op — restricted forum content is searchable by anonymous users

**Статус: Исправлено.** Предикат стал (роль ИЛИ пользователь) И НЕ запрещен; личность искателя проброшена в воркер через proto, а не бралась из его гостевой ambient-идентичности; исправлен HasFlag по не-флаговому enum. Проверено анонимным запросом до и после (0be01dc9)

**Доказательство.** SearchEngineRepository.cs:60-63 builds the access filter as a three-way OR: `q.Terms(AuthorizedRoles, roles) || q.Terms(AuthorizedUsers, userId) || !q.Terms(UnauthorizedUsers, userId)`. The third clause is a bool must_not against `UnauthorizedUsers`, and `UnauthorizedUsers` is written by NO indexer — grep across src/ returns only its declaration (SearchEntity.cs:50), the _source exclusion (SearchEngineRepository.cs:38) and this query clause. An empty array never matches a terms query, so must_not matches every document, so the whole OR is unconditionally true. Independently, IdentityInterceptor.cs:16 hardcodes `identitySetter.Current = Identity.Guest()` on every gRPC call, and SearchController.cs:65-70 propagates no identity metadata at all — so even a correct filter would evaluate against Guest for every caller. SearchController has no [Authorize] and Startup.cs declares no FallbackPoli

**Последствие.** The moment any board is given a restricted ViewPolicy (the enum supports Administrator/SeniorModerator/Moderator/Mentor tiers — BoardAccessPolicy.cs:19-39 — and boards are admin-editable), its topics and comments become fully searchable by unauthenticated visitors, complete with <mark>-highlighted text fragments. Today's seed happens to set every board to ViewPolicy=64 (InitialCreate.cs:1670-1680), so nothing leaks yet — this is a loaded gun, not a smoking one. A secondary effect is already live: every search runs with Guest paging settings (SearchService.cs:22-23), ignoring user preferences.

**Исправление.** Delete `UnauthorizedUsers` (dead field, never written) and make the filter `AuthorizedRoles ∩ callerRoles OR AuthorizedUsers ∋ callerId` with no escape clause. Propagate the caller's identity from SearchController into the gRPC call as metadata and have IdentityInterceptor reconstruct it instead of hardcoding Guest. Add a test that indexes a Moderator-only topic and asserts a Guest search returns zero hits.

**Проверка оппонентом.** MECHANISM VERIFIED — I could not refute it. D:\Code Projects\dm3\src\DM.Infrastructure.Core\Search\SearchEngineRepository.cs:60-63 is literally `q.Terms(AuthorizedRoles, roles) || q.Terms(AuthorizedUsers, userId) || !q.Terms(UnauthorizedUsers, userId)` — an OR, not an AND. `UnauthorizedUsers` is written by nothing: repo-wide grep returns only the declaration (DM.Domain.Core/Search/SearchEntity.cs:50), the _source exclusion (SearchEngineRepository.cs:38) and this clause; all eight indexers set only AuthorizedRoles/AuthorizedUsers. I checked the obvious escape hatch — OpenSearch.Client conditionless-query elision would drop the clause and accidentally save it — but it does not apply: `.Terms(userId)` is a single non-null Guid, so the terms query is writable, `!` emits a real bool.must_not, the field is AutoMapped (IndexingRepository.cs:61) yet empty on every document, so must_not matches a

### [КРИТИЧНО] PG-02 — MinIO garbage collector can never match a row — deleted files are never removed from object storage

**Статус: Исправлено.** IgnoreQueryFilters + DeletedUtc, 3 теста red-green (9ccbabc2)

**Доказательство.** Upload implements ISoftDeletable (Entities/Shared/Upload.cs:14), ISoftDeletable extends IRemovable (Contracts/ISoftDeletable.cs:8), and DmDbContext.cs:598-607 attaches a global query filter `!IsRemoved` to every IRemovable entity. UploadOrphanCleanupService.cs:88-92 then queries `db.Uploads.Where(u => u.IsRemoved && u.DeletedUtc != null && u.DeletedUtc < cutoff)` with no `IgnoreQueryFilters()` — and `IgnoreQueryFilters` appears nowhere in the entire src/ tree. The generated SQL is `WHERE NOT "IsRemoved" AND "IsRemoved" AND ...`: always empty. The service is registered and running (Startup.cs:165) and logs "No orphans to remove" forever. Compounding it, UploadApiService.DeleteUpload:148 sets `upload.IsRemoved = true` and never sets DeletedUtc, so even with the filter fixed those rows fail the `DeletedUtc != null` predicate. UserRepository.UnlinkAvatarUpload:464-476 carries the comment "Th

**Последствие.** Every avatar a user replaces or deletes, and every attachment a moderator removes, stays in MinIO indefinitely at its original public URL with `Cache-Control: immutable` (UploadApiService.cs:420). Moderation removal of illegal or abusive imagery does not actually remove the file — it only hides the row. Storage and the Uploads table grow monotonically forever; the 24h "grace period" restore window is fiction in the other direction too.

**Исправление.** Add `.IgnoreQueryFilters()` to the sweeper query, set `DeletedUtc = _dateTimeProvider.Now` in UploadApiService.DeleteUpload alongside IsRemoved, and add a regression test that soft-deletes an upload, advances the clock past the grace period, runs one sweep, and asserts the S3 delete was issued and the row is gone.

**Проверка оппонентом.** REFUTATION FAILED — every cited line is accurate and the bug is unconditional, not a race.

Verified verbatim:
1. D:\Code Projects\dm3\src\DM.Infrastructure.Persistence\Entities\Shared\Upload.cs:14 — `public class Upload : ISoftDeletable`.
2. ...\Entities\Contracts\ISoftDeletable.cs:8 — `internal interface ISoftDeletable : IRemovable`.
3. ...\DmDbContext.cs:598-607 — the loop over `modelBuilder.Model.GetEntityTypes()` applies `HasQueryFilter(e => !e.IsRemoved)` to every `IRemovable` CLR type; it is the LAST statement in OnModelCreating, so nothing overrides it. `HasQueryFilter` appears exactly once in the file (line 605), and `Uploads` is a plain `DbSet<Upload>` (line 718).
4. ...\src\DM.Web.API\HostedServices\UploadOrphanCleanupService.cs:88-92 — `db.Uploads.Where(u => u.IsRemoved && u.DeletedUtc != null && u.DeletedUtc < cutoff)`, no `IgnoreQueryFilters()`. A repo-wide grep for `Ignore

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] PG-03 — The Outbox pattern is documented as the delivery guarantee but has zero writers — every cross-store event is a fire-and-forget dual write with no rebuild path

**Статус: Частично.** То же, что P-10 (1876e028)

**Доказательство.** DATA_STORAGE.md:123-124 states the outbox guarantees "что событие не потеряется даже при сбое". The table exists (DmDbContext.cs:723), the processor is fully built with exponential backoff and dead-lettering (OutboxProcessor.cs:83-131), it is registered and running (Startup.cs:372), and it even has a unit test (test/DM.Infrastructure.Messaging.Tests/OutboxProcessorShould.cs). But grep for `OutboxEvents.Add` / `new OutboxEvent` across src/ returns nothing outside the DbSet declaration and migrations — nothing ever inserts a row. Domain services publish directly: GameService.cs:844 `return _producer.SendAsync(EventType.NewGame, gameId)` via InvokedEventProducer.cs:23, after SaveChanges, outside any transaction. Separately, there is no reindex or backfill command anywhere (grep 'reindex|backfill' across src/, docs/, scripts/ finds only unrelated comment counters), and forum topics are seede

**Последствие.** If RabbitMQ is unavailable, or the process dies between SaveChangesAsync and Send, the OpenSearch document is silently never written and nothing ever notices or repairs it. Because there is no reindex path, divergence is permanent — losing the `osdata` volume means search returns nothing for all pre-existing content forever. The running OutboxProcessor is pure noise: a background poller over a table that is empty by construction.

**Исправление.** Either wire the producers through the outbox (write the OutboxEvent row inside the same SaveChanges as the aggregate, let the processor relay it) or delete the table, the processor, its registration and its test and stop documenting a guarantee the system does not provide. Independently, add a reindex command that streams Topics/Comments/Users from Postgres into a fresh alias — without it OpenSearch is an unrecoverable store.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Every citation verified against the files; I found no guard, config, or deliberate-design rationale that defuses it.

WHAT HOLDS:
1. Zero writers, confirmed exhaustively. `grep -rn "OutboxEvent" src/ --include=*.cs` returns exactly 12 hits: the DbSet (DmDbContext.cs:723), the entity (Entities/Shared/OutboxEvent.cs:10), the reader (OutboxProcessor.cs:70), and migration/snapshot noise. No `OutboxEvents.Add`, no `new OutboxEvent`, and no `ISaveChangesInterceptor`/`SaveChangesInterceptor` anywhere in src/ (grep returns nothing) — so there is no implicit writer either. The table is empty by construction.
2. It is registered and running. MessageQueuingModule.cs:49-54 registers `OutboxProcessor` `.As<IHostedService>()` when the flag is set, and Startup.cs:372 sets it: `new MessageQueuingModule(enableOutboxProcessor: !_migrateOnStart)`. So DM.Web.API polls an always-empty table every 5s (OutboxC

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] PG-04 — Six of the twelve declared Mongo indexes reference fields that do not exist; the hottest query — session lookup on every authenticated request — is a full collection scan

**Статус: Исправлено.** 6 индексов по несуществующим полям заменены; Sessions._id закрыл COLLSCAN на каждом запросе; создание перенесено в приложение (75e5c26d)

**Доказательство.** docker/mongo-init.js declares indexes whose keys do not match the documents. `UserSessions` gets `{UserId:1, IsActive:1}` and `{ExpirationDate:1}` (lines 49-57) but the document is `{ _id (Id), Sessions: [{ Id, ExpirationUtc, ... }] }` (Entities/Account/UserSession.cs:17-39) — no UserId, no IsActive, no ExpirationDate field exists. `RealtimeNotifications` gets `{UserId, IsRead, CreateDate}` and `{UserId, IsRemoved}` (lines 76-84) but Notification has none of those four fields; it has `UsersInterested[]`, `UsersNotified[]`, `CreatedUtc` (Entities/Personal/Notifications/Notification.cs:24-34). `Polls` gets `{TopicId:1}` (line 92) but Poll has no TopicId at all (Entities/Forum/Poll.cs:18-56). Meanwhile the actually-hot query, AuthenticationRepository.FindUserSession:60-63 `ElemMatch(u => u.Sessions, s => s.Id == sessionId)`, needs an index on `Sessions.Id` — which is not declared. Neither a

**Последствие.** Session validation — which runs on every authenticated request — COLLSCANs UserSessions. The notification list (NotificationRepository.cs:24-47, filtering `UsersInterested` and sorting `CreatedUtc`) COLLSCANs RealtimeNotifications. Six declared indexes consume write amplification and disk while indexing nothing but nulls. Worse, `mongo-init.js` only executes on first startup of an empty data volume, so no correction can ever reach an existing deployment.

**Исправление.** Correct the key specs to `{'Sessions.Id':1}`, `{'Sessions.ExpirationUtc':1}`, `{UsersInterested:1, CreatedUtc:-1}`, `{UsersNotified:1}`, add `{UserId:1, TimestampUtc:-1}` on SecurityAuditLog and LoginAttempts, and drop the Polls TopicId index. Then move index assertion into the application (an idempotent `Indexes.CreateManyAsync` at startup, like DeclareIndex does for OpenSearch) so it applies to existing volumes instead of only to fresh ones.

**Проверка оппонентом.** I could not refute it — every load-bearing citation checks out against the files. docker/mongo-init.js:49-57 indexes UserSessions on {UserId,IsActive} and {ExpirationDate}, but UserSession.cs:11-22 is {Id (→_id), Sessions[]} and Session (lines 28-68) exposes Id/ExpirationUtc/Persistent/Invisible/CreatedUtc/IpAddress/UserAgent/DeviceInfo — none of the three indexed fields exist. mongo-init.js:76-84 indexes RealtimeNotifications on {UserId,IsRead,CreateDate} and {UserId,IsRemoved}, while Notification.cs:12-47 has NotificationId/CreatedUtc/UsersInterested/UsersNotified/Metadata/EventType — zero overlap. mongo-init.js:92-95 indexes Polls on TopicId, absent from Poll.cs:12-53. The hot path is real and uncached: AuthenticationRepository.cs:60-63 does ElemMatch(u => u.Sessions, s => s.Id == sessionId) with no Sessions.Id index, reached from AuthenticationService.cs:160 via Middleware/Authentica

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] PG-06 — Games are never indexed — the search index is created without its mapping, and NewGame is not routed to the indexer

**Статус: Исправлено.** Снято вместе с движком: индексировать больше нечем и незачем. При разборе выяснилось, что находка недооценила масштаб - не индексировались не только игры. NewUserIndexer не проставлял ни AuthorizedRoles, ни AuthorizedUsers, а фильтр авторизации требует попадания хотя бы в одно из множеств, поэтому пользователи не находились никогда. Из четырех заявленных типов общий поиск в бою мог отдать только темы и комментарии - ровно те два, что переехали на tsvector

**Доказательство.** Two independent breakages in the same worker. (1) SearchIndexerConsumer.cs:54-66 subscribes to exactly seven routing keys — ActivatedUser, NewTopicComment, ChangedTopicComment, DeletedTopicComment, NewTopic, ChangedTopic, DeletedTopic. `EventType.NewGame` is absent, so NewGameIndexer.cs (a complete, registered, DI-resolved class) never fires. GameService.cs:844 does publish NewGame; NotificationDispatcherConsumer.cs:68 subscribes to it, so the event is live — it just never reaches the search indexer. SearchController.cs:18 and 39 advertise search "across users, games, and posts". (2) SearchIndexerConsumer.cs:43-51 unconditionally creates the index at worker startup with a bare `Indices.Create(IndexName)` — no mapping, no analyzer. IndexingRepository.DeclareIndex:44-67, which carries the real definition (dm_analyzer with html_strip, dm_search_analyzer, the Text field mapping), short-circu

**Последствие.** Searching for a game by title or description returns nothing, ever, while the API documents it as supported. And because the index is created bare, OpenSearch assigns dynamic mappings from the first indexed document: the carefully specified html_strip char filter never applies, so raw HTML tags emitted by `parser.Parse(...).ToHtml()` (NewTopicIndexer.cs:49) become searchable tokens — a query for "br" or "div" matches most of the forum.

**Исправление.** Add `EventType.NewGame` (and the corresponding Changed/Deleted game events, once indexers exist) to the RoutingKeys array. Delete the index-creation block in SearchIndexerConsumer entirely and let IndexingRepository.DeclareIndex own it — one place creates the index, and it is the place that knows the mapping.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Both sub-claims verified in source; no guard, config, or documented intent rescues them. (1) SearchIndexerConsumer.cs:57-66 binds queue dm.search-engine to exactly seven literal routing keys (community.user.activated, forum.topic.*, forum.topic.comment.*). EventType.cs:507-508 assigns NewGame the literal key "game.created", published at GameService.cs:844 via InvokedEventProducer.cs:23-28 into the "dm.events" exchange. I checked the refutation that the exchange might be fanout: Jamq.Client.Rabbit 0.10.0 contains exactly one exchange-type string literal, "topic", and neither SearchIndexerConsumer nor NotificationDispatcherConsumer sets ExchangeType, so routing keys do filter and no wildcard covers game.created. NewGameIndexer.cs:33 is genuinely live in DI (ModuleRegistrationExtensions.cs:88-101 registers non-abstract classes AsImplementedInterfaces, so it lands in CompositeIndexer.cs:30-3

## Опровергнуто оппонентом (в работу не берем)

- **PG-05** — Deleting an attribute schema hard-deletes the Mongo document while Postgres rows still reference it, breaking every game that used it. Причина отклонения: The code reading is accurate but the impact is unreachable, so the "high" claim collapses.

VERIFIED AS DESCRIBED (mechanism): AttributeSchemaRepository.Delete is a hard delete (src/DM.Infrastructure.Persistence/Repositories/Game/AttributeSchemaRepository.cs:188-189) despite IsRemoved existing on the entity (src/DM.Infrastructure.Persistence/Entities/Game/Characters/Attributes/AttributeSchema.cs:4

## Средние и низкие

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] PG-07 — The PG/Mongo split is not principled — one feature is split across both stores, and the same document is created in four places with two different sets of defaults

**Статус: Исправлено.** Фича черного списка переехала в Postgres целиком. Записи и так были реляционными - таблица UserBlacklists с внешним ключом и уникальным индексом, - а выключатель поведения лежал в документе Mongo UserSettings.BlacklistSettings. Из-за этого вопрос 'заблокирован ли пользователь и действует ли это здесь' был двумя чтениями в двух базах без транзакции и без способа их согласовать. Флаги перенесены колонкой User.BlacklistSettings, репозиторий больше не наследует MongoCollectionRepository и не берет DmMongoClient, GetBlockedUserIdsIfFlagEnabledAsync стал одним запросом. Правило 'одна фича живет в одном хранилище целиком' в DATA_STORAGE.md уже записано - теперь оно выполняется. Миграция перегенерирована, три полиморфных FK сняты по регламенту, 278 интеграционных тестов зеленые

DATA_STORAGE.md:23-30 gives four justifications for Mongo (nested arrays, high write volume, schemaless, per-type fields). The blacklist feature satisfies none of them yet lives in both stores: entries are Postgres rows with a unique index (DmDbContext.cs:463-464, UserBlacklistRepository.cs:37-116) while the three on/off flags are a field inside the Mongo UserSettings document (UserBlacklistRepository.cs:119-133) — so `GetBlockedUserIdsIfFlagEnabledAsync` costs one Mongo round-trip plus one Postgres query per call. Because the UserSettings document has no owner, four separate sites lazily create it with hand-written defaults: UserSettings.Default at Identity/UserSettings.cs:29-38 (all 10), BotLinkRepository.cs:143 (10), UserBlacklistRepository.cs:159-166 (10), and UserRepository.UpdateUser:356-363 (20/20/20/20/10). Registration creates no settings document at all (grep of DM.Domain.Account/Features/Registration finds no UserSettings reference).

Последствие: Which per-page defaults a user gets depends on which endpoint happened to materialize their settings document first: edit your profile and you get 20 topics per page, toggle a blacklist flag and you get 10. There is no schema, no migration and no single authority to fix it after the fact. More broadly, the store boundary here is historical accident rather than the documented rule, which makes the rule unenforceable for the next feature.

Исправление: Move BlacklistSettings into the Postgres UserBlacklist scope (or a Users column) so the feature lives in one store, and create the UserSettings document once at registration from `UserSettings.Default`, deleting the three ad-hoc constructors. If lazy creation must stay, make a single `EnsureSettings(userId)` the only writer.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] PG-08 — The soft-delete contract is silently enforced only in Postgres; Mongo repositories must hand-write it and half of them forget

**Статус: Исправлено.** Разобрано по частям, а не мини-ORM над Mongo ради трех коллекций. Настоящий дефект один и он починен: FlushAsync и FlushAllAsync искали счетчик без условия IsRemoved, поэтому 'отметить прочитанным' у удаленной сущности находило ее надгробие и upsert-ом воскрешал живую строку - сущность возвращалась в сайдбар. Заодно снят ложный контракт: UnreadCounter объявлял ISoftDeletable, но DeletedByUserId и DeletedUtc не писал никто; переведен на IRemovable, мертвые поля удалены

DmDbContext.cs:598-607 applies `HasQueryFilter(e => !e.IsRemoved)` to every IRemovable entity — invisible, automatic, correct. Mongo documents declare the same interfaces (AttributeSchema : IRemovable, Entities/.../AttributeSchema.cs:13; UnreadCounter : ISoftDeletable, Entities/Shared/UnreadCounter.cs:14; Poll : IRemovable, Entities/Forum/Poll.cs:13) but get no such enforcement — MongoCollectionRepository (MongoIntegration/MongoCollectionRepository.cs:20-40) exposes raw Filter/Update builders with no base predicate. The result is inconsistent by repository: PollRepository remembers (`Filter.Eq(p => p.IsRemoved, false)` at lines 100 and 182), UnreadCountersRepository remembers in the aggregations (lines 105, 134, 163) but forgets in FlushAsync:178-181 and FlushAllAsync:203-206 and ChangeParentAsync:229-232, and AttributeSchemaRepository never filters it at all (GetSchemata:54-56, GetSchema:86-88) while hard-deleting instead.

Последствие: The same `IRemovable` annotation means "guaranteed filtered" on one entity and "filtered if the author remembered" on the next, with nothing in the type system or a test to tell them apart. Soft-deleted UnreadCounters are resurrected by Flush upserts. The bug class is invisible at the call site — a reviewer reading `Collection.Find(...)` has no signal that a predicate is missing.

Исправление: Give MongoCollectionRepository<TEntity> a base filter that ANDs `IsRemoved == false` automatically when TEntity : IRemovable, with an explicit `IncludingRemoved()` escape hatch — mirroring the EF global filter plus IgnoreQueryFilters. Or stop implementing IRemovable on Mongo documents so the contract does not imply a guarantee it cannot provide.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] PG-09 — DATA_STORAGE.md never says how to tell a Mongo document from a Postgres table, and the code gives no visible signal either

**Статус: Открыто.** 

The doc's "Организация Entities" section (DATA_STORAGE.md:34-74) prescribes the folder layout and a decision procedure for *which module* an entity belongs to, but says nothing about which physical store it lands in. In the tree the two kinds are interleaved with no separation: Entities/Account/ holds User.cs and Token.cs (Postgres) next to UserSession.cs and Settings/UserSettings.cs (Mongo); Entities/Forum/ holds Board.cs and Topic.cs (Postgres) next to Poll.cs (Mongo); Entities/Shared/ holds Upload.cs and OutboxEvent.cs (Postgres) next to UnreadCounter.cs (Mongo). The only discriminator is `[MongoCollectionName]` — and MongoCollectionNameAttribute is declared `internal` (MongoIntegration/MongoCollectionNameAttribute.cs:9), so it is not even visible from the domain assemblies that consume these types. Nothing enforces that a Mongo document is absent from DmDbContext's DbSets.

Последствие: An engineer adding a field, an index, or a query cannot tell which store they are touching without opening the file and scanning for an attribute. This is exactly the ambiguity that produced findings PG-04 (indexes written against the wrong field names), PG-05 (hard delete on a cross-store-referenced document) and PG-08 (inconsistent soft-delete). The document that calls itself the SSOT for storage organization omits the one fact that matters most.

Исправление: Either separate the trees (Entities/Relational/{Module}/ vs Entities/Documents/{Module}/) or state the discriminator explicitly in DATA_STORAGE.md and make MongoCollectionNameAttribute public so the marker is legible everywhere. Add an architecture test asserting that no type carrying [MongoCollectionName] appears in DmDbContext's model.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] PG-10 — Four stores for a hobby RPG site: OpenSearch does a strictly easier job worse than the tsvector search already in the codebase

**Статус: Исправлено.** OpenSearch снят с поиска по решению владельца, вместе с ним ушел весь проект-воркер DM.Workers.SearchIndexer, gRPC-контракт и клиент, пакеты OpenSearch.Client и пять пакетов Grpc.*. Общий поиск переписан на Postgres tsvector: generated-колонки с GIN на Topics (Title весом A, Text весом B через setweight) и Comments, эндпоинт GET /v1/search/forum, доступ выводится из существующего предиката видимости доски на каждом запросе, а не пишется в документ при индексации. Довод владельца подтвердился на практике: анализатор OpenSearch был html_strip+standard+lowercase+stop, без русской морфологии, а tsvector идет через to_tsvector('russian') - интеграционный тест находит 'странников' по запросу 'странник'. Логи Serilog остаются на OpenSearch до отдельной правки

OpenSearch indexes exactly three entity kinds — users, forum topics, forum comments (the seven routing keys at SearchIndexerConsumer.cs:57-66). Postgres already implements a harder search correctly: MessageSearchRepository does full-text over messages *and* game posts with dynamic per-caller access, private-block stripping, keyset pagination and GIN indexes (DmDbContext.cs:231-249, MessageSearchRepository.cs:53-118). Public forum content has none of those complications. The OpenSearch path costs: a whole worker service (DM.Workers.SearchIndexer), a gRPC contract and generated Protos, CompositeIndexer plus eight indexer classes, IndexingRepository, SearchEngineRepository, SearchService, an interceptor, plus opensearch + opensearch-dashboards containers reserving 1G and 512M (docker/docker-compose.yml:159, 185). It carries findings PG-01, PG-03 and PG-06 by itself. Mongo similarly holds nine collections of which only three (Dice, AttributeSchemata, Polls) are genuinely document-shaped; UserSessions, UserSettings, LoginAttempts, SecurityAuditLog and UnreadCounters are flat rows that Postgres would model with FKs, TTL partitions and real constraints.

Последствие: Four stores means four backup/restore procedures, four failure modes, four index-management stories and — as this review found — three of them silently broken with no test coverage (grep of test/ finds no Mongo, no OpenSearch and no upload-GC tests; the only relevant test covers the outbox processor, which processes an empty table). Honest cost/benefit: dropping OpenSearch and folding the flat Mongo collections into Postgres would delete one worker service, one gRPC contract, ~15 files and ~1.5G of container reservation, and would eliminate PG-01, PG-03, PG-04 and PG-06 outright — while *improving* search, because the tsvector implementation already handles the harder case.

Исправление: Extend the existing tsvector search to Topics and Comments (a generated column plus GIN index, same shape as Message.SearchVector) and retire OpenSearch and the indexer worker. Keep Mongo only for Dice, AttributeSchemata and Polls if the document shapes are worth a second store — otherwise jsonb columns cover all three at this scale. If the polyglot setup is deliberately kept as a learning exercise, say so in DATA_STORAGE.md; right now the doc presents it as a requirements-driven choice.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] PG-11 — DmMongoClient is registered per-dependency, and the security audit log in Mongo has no index, no TTL and no retention

**Статус: Исправлено.** Обе половины. DmMongoClient переведен в SingleInstance - клиент владеет пулом соединений и монитором кластера, регистрация per-dependency выдавала каждому потребителю свой и обессмысливала пул. Коллекция SecurityAuditLog получила составной индекс (UserId, TimestampUtc desc) под единственную форму чтения и TTL на 180 дней; журнал хранит адреса и user-agent, то есть персональные данные, и бессрочность тут не польза, а обязательство. Индексы отзеркалены в mongo-init.js, счетчик 12 -> 14

PersistenceModule.cs:102-115 registers DmMongoClient with `builder.Register(...).AsSelf().AsImplementedInterfaces()` and no lifetime — Autofac's default is InstancePerDependency, so a new MongoClient is constructed on every resolution.
