- `src/DM.Web.API/Shared/Authentication/WebAuthenticationService.cs:63-76` — `ExtractClientIp` reads `Headers["X-Forwarded-For"].FirstOrDefault()`, splits on ',', takes `.First()` (comment literally says "Take the first IP (client)"), validates only with `IPAddress.TryParse` (any syntactically valid IP passes), and falls back to `RemoteIpAddress` only when the header is absent/unparseable.
- `src/DM.Web.API/Features/Account/Authentication/AuthenticationApiService.cs:156-169` — byte-identical copy (only `System.StringSplitOptions` vs `StringSplitOptions` differ).
- `docker/nginx/nginx.conf:76` (also :88, :101, :111) — `proxy_set_header X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for`, which appends the peer to the client-supplied value; there is no `real_ip_module` / `set_real_ip_from` anywh

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] SEC-09 — Bot webhook endpoint fails open when its secret is unconfigured — which is the shipped default

**Статус: Исправлено.** Fail-closed + сравнение за фиксированное время (24a43d5e)

**Доказательство.** `Features/Personal/Webhooks/WebhookController.cs:73-78`: `// In dev mode (no secret configured), accept any request` / `if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(expectedSecret) && secret != expectedSecret) return 403;`. When `BotConfiguration.TelegramWebhookSecret` / `DiscordWebhookSecret` is empty the guard is skipped entirely. `appsettings.json` ships `BotConfiguration` with empty tokens and no webhook secrets at all, and `docker-compose.yml` sets no `DM_BotConfiguration__*`. The handler then reaches `BotLinkService.VerifyAndLink(code, channelType, externalId)` (`TelegramWebhookHandler.cs:58`), which links an arbitrary attacker-supplied `chatId` to whichever account owns the 6-character code (`BotLinkService.cs:65-88`). The code is the first 6 hex chars of a GUID — 24 bits — matched by prefix lookup, and the endpoint is unauthenticated with endpoint rate limiting inert (SEC-04). The comparison at `:7

**Последствие.** On a default deployment any anonymous party can POST forged bot updates and, with a feasible brute force of a 24-bit space against a shared 100 req/min bucket, bind their own Telegram/Discord chat to a victim's account and receive that account's notification stream. Fail-open-when-unconfigured is the wrong default for an authentication check.

**Исправление.** Invert the default: reject with 403 when the expected secret is null or empty, and validate with `CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals`. Require the secret via `ValidateOnStart` if the bot feature is enabled. Widen the link code to at least 128 bits of entropy and rate-limit redemption attempts per external id.

**Проверка оппонентом.** REFUTATION ATTEMPT FAILED ON THE CORE CLAIM — every load-bearing fact checks out verbatim. `src/DM.Web.API/Features/Personal/Webhooks/WebhookController.cs:72-77` is exactly as quoted: the 403 is emitted only `if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(expectedSecret) && secret != expectedSecret)`, so an unset secret skips the guard entirely. The secret is genuinely unset everywhere shipped: `src/DM.Web.API/appsettings.json:59-62` has only `DiscordBotToken`/`TelegramBotToken` (both empty), `appsettings.template.json:76-79` likewise, and `docker/docker-compose.yml` sets no `DM_BotConfiguration__*` (grep over `docker/` returns nothing). The keys exist in NO config file at all — an operator has no cue they exist without reading `src/DM.Infrastructure.Core/Configuration/BotConfiguration.cs:44,51`. The endpoint is genuinely anonymous: no `[Authorize]`, and `Middleware/CsrfProtectionMiddleware.cs:65-69` explici

## Средние и низкие

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] SEC-10 — Security headers are applied to the API origin only; the SPA origin serves no CSP, no X-Frame-Options, no nosniff

**Статус: Исправлено.** Уточнение к находке: nosniff, X-Frame-Options, Referrer-Policy и Permissions-Policy на HTML-страницы доходят - их ставит краевой прокси на уровне server. Не хватало ровно CSP, и он добавлен в location / клиентского nginx (только там: CSP имеет смысл на документе, а блок статики объявляет свой add_header и отменил бы наследование). Состав директив выведен из того, что реально грузит сборка: инлайновый anti-FOUC скрипт и javascript:void(0) у спойлеров требуют unsafe-inline для скриптов, рантайм-инъекция стилей Vue и TipTap - для стилей, картинки открыты широко (imgproxy/CDN на настраиваемом origin, data: у дефолтного аватара, blob: в компрессоре, произвольный [img] в пользовательском BBCode), iframe и медиа запрещены - парсер BBCode их не выпускает. Попутно снят конфликт X-Frame-Options: middleware отдавал DENY, прокси SAMEORIGIN, add_header не заменяет а добавляет - браузер при расхождении игнорирует директиву целиком. Прокси приведен к DENY, как обещает документация. Дубль таблицы заголовков из AUTHENTICATION.md заменен ссылкой на SECURITY.md

`SecurityHeadersMiddleware.cs:39-73` decorates responses from `DM.Web.API` — i.e. `application/json` payloads, where `X-Frame-Options`, `frame-ancestors` and `script-src` have essentially no meaning. The HTML application is served by a separate container whose config is `src/DM.Web.Client/nginx.conf`: 19 lines, `try_files` plus a cache rule, and not a single `add_header`. `docker/nginx/nginx.conf:52-56` adds four headers at the edge but no `Content-Security-Policy`, and downgrades `X-Frame-Options` to `SAMEORIGIN`. Additionally the API's own CSP (`:67`) contains `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`, which permits exactly the inline event handlers that SEC-01 injects.

Последствие: `docs/conventions/SECURITY.md:58-68` and `AUTHENTICATION.md:121-131` both assert a `default-src 'self'` CSP and clickjacking protection as delivered controls. Neither reaches a single HTML page. The defense-in-depth layer that should have blunted SEC-01 and SEC-06 does not exist, and the documentation actively misleads the next reviewer into thinking it does.

Исправление: Move the header policy to whatever serves HTML — `src/DM.Web.Client/nginx.conf` and/or the edge — and drop `'unsafe-inline'` from `script-src` (Vite builds do not need it; use nonces or hashes if some inline bootstrap remains). Keep `nosniff` on the API. Then correct the two docs to say which origin each header applies to.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] SEC-11 — CSRF protection is a single Origin check that fails open, with no antiforgery token behind it

**Статус: Отклонено.** Браузер всегда шлет Origin на межсайтовый POST, куки SameSite=Lax; fail-closed сломает dm.ps1 seed, польза нулевая

`Middleware/CsrfProtectionMiddleware.cs:65-69`: when both `Origin` and `Referer` are absent the request is passed through, on the reasoning in the comment at `:62-64` that such callers "can't carry auth cookies anyway" — which is false for any non-browser client replaying a stolen cookie, and false for browser edge cases such as proxy-stripped headers. There is no `IAntiforgery` usage anywhere in the solution (grep: zero hits). The class docblock at `:16-19` also states "the API uses custom auth headers (not cookies)", which contradicts the actual BFF cookie design in `ApiCredentialsStorage.cs`. `SameSite=Lax` was chosen over `Strict` specifically for the mirror transfer flow (`:59`) that SEC-07 shows is not implemented.

Последствие: The whole CSRF posture rests on one header comparison with a permissive default plus `SameSite=Lax`. Modern browsers do send `Origin` on state-changing requests, so this is not immediately exploitable — but there is no second layer, the stated justification for the weaker `SameSite` value is obsolete, and the middleware's own documentation describes a different authentication model than the one in use.

Исправление: Make the missing-Origin case reject rather than allow for cookie-authenticated requests (an explicit allow-list for server-to-server callers if needed). Switch the cookie to `SameSite=Strict` now that the transfer flow is gone, or add a double-submit token if `Lax` must stay. Correct the middleware docblock.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] SEC-12 — Account enumeration oracle on /v1/account/recovery, contradicting the endpoint's own documentation

**Статус: Принято как исключение.** Перечисление неизбежно при удобной регистрации: три surface записаны в SECURITY.md, ложные утверждения убраны, каждый раскрывающий ответ логируется (b627a6b6)

`Features/Account/Recovery/RecoveryApiService.cs:38-59` maps `RecoveryResult.NotFound` to `RecoveryStatus.NotFound` and returns it to the caller, alongside `PasswordResetSent` and `ActivationResent`. `RecoveryController.cs:47` documents the opposite: "**Not found**: Returns success to prevent enumeration". `docs/conventions/SECURITY.md:80` lists "Не раскрывать существование пользователей (одинаковые ответы)" as a mandatory checklist item. The login path leaks the same information through a different channel: `AuthenticationApiService.cs:98-115` returns distinct 403s for `Banned` and `Removed` and a distinct 400 carrying `_pendingActivation: true` for a pending registration, versus a generic 400 for wrong credentials — and `AuthenticationService.cs:66-70` performs the pending check *before* any throttling.

Последствие: Any email address can be classified as registered / pending / banned / unknown in one request. With endpoint rate limiting inert (SEC-04), the full user base is enumerable, which is the precursor step for both credential stuffing and targeted lockout (SEC-13). Three separate places in the codebase claim this is prevented.

Исправление: Collapse the recovery response to a single status regardless of outcome. Return the identical error shape for `Banned`, `Removed` and `PendingRegistration` on login and surface the real reason only through the emailed link. If `check-email` must stay as a registration-UX affordance, accept it as a deliberate exception and say so in `SECURITY.md` instead of claiming blanket non-disclosure.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] SEC-13 — Lockout is keyed on email alone, giving a targeted denial-of-service on any account

**Статус: Исправлено.** Счетчик неудачных попыток ключуется парой (аккаунт, адрес) вместо одного email: 15 неверных паролей больше не запирают произвольного пользователя на полчаса откуда угодно. Композиция ключа живет в одном типе LoginAttemptOrigin, а не размазана по репозиторию. Успешный вход чистит записи со всех адресов сразу - для этого email денормализован из составного _id. Добавлены индекс по Email и TTL-индекс: CleanupExpiredRecords существовал, но не вызывался ниоткуда, поэтому счетчик не затухал и 15 опечаток за полгода запирали аккаунт на ровном месте

`AuthenticationService.cs:73-88` calls `_loginAttemptTracker.IsAccountLocked(email)` and every tracker operation in `LoginAttemptTracker.cs:32-90` is keyed by email only — `RecordFailedAttempt(email)` at `:75-84` sets a lockout once the count crosses `AccountLockoutThreshold` (15 per `docs/conventions/SECURITY.md:49`), for `AccountLockoutDurationMinutes` (30). No IP or device dimension participates. The progressive delay at `:92-115` is likewise per-email, and `Task.Delay` is applied server-side (`AuthenticationService.cs:91-95`), holding a request thread and a DB connection for up to 30s per attempt.

Последствие: 15 deliberately wrong passwords lock any user — including every moderator and admin — out for 30 minutes, repeatable indefinitely and cheaply given SEC-04 and SEC-12. The server-side sleep also turns the throttle into an amplification vector: an attacker can pin request slots by triggering delays against arbitrary emails.

Исправление: Key lockout on (email, client IP) and keep a separate, much wider per-IP counter to catch spraying. Do not sleep the request thread — return 429 with `Retry-After` instead, which is both cheaper and clearer to the client.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] SEC-14 — SignalR accepts the session token from a query-string parameter

**Статус: Исправлено.** Фолбэк на access_token в query-строке удален. Потребителей у него не было: SPA подключается с withCredentials и без accessTokenFactory, других клиентов хаба в репозитории нет. Зато был полноценный сессионный токен в URL, который прокси пишет в access_log дословно, обесценивая HttpOnly-куку, ради которой существует BFF. Документация приведена в соответствие

`Notifications/NotificationHub.cs:81-85`: after trying the `dm_session` cookie, `TryExtractAuthToken` falls back to `httpContext.Request.Query["access_token"]` and passes it to `_connectionService.Add(token, …)`, which authenticates it as a full session token. The hub itself is `[AllowAnonymous]` (`:27`).

Последствие: A credential equivalent to the session cookie is accepted in a URL, where it is written to nginx access logs (`docker/nginx/nginx.conf` logs by default), browser history and any intermediary. This voids the HttpOnly/Secure guarantees the BFF design exists to provide, for a "non-browser clients" case that has no identified consumer in this codebase.

Исправление: Remove the query-parameter fallback — the browser client already sends the cookie on the negotiate and upgrade requests. If a non-cookie transport is genuinely needed later, use a short-lived, single-purpose hub ticket rather than the session token.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] SEC-15 — Unhandled exception messages are returned to the client in 500 responses

**Статус: Исправлено.** То же, что API-06 (2107db2b)

`Middleware/ExceptionProblemDetailsFactoryExtensions.cs:62-65`: the fallback overload passes `exception.Message` as the ProblemDetails *title* for every unhandled exception, alongside a correlation token in `detail`. `ErrorHandlingMiddleware.cs:71-74` routes all non-`HttpException` exceptions there.

Последствие: Npgsql, EF Core, AutoMapper and MongoDB driver messages routinely embed table and column names, parameter values, internal type names and occasionally connection details. That is free reconnaissance for an attacker probing for injection or misconfiguration, and it is exactly the class of response that ends up in a bug bounty report.

Исправление: Return a fixed title ("Internal server error") plus the correlation token; log `exception.Message` and the stack server-side only. Keep the current behaviour for `IsDevelopment()` if it helps local debugging.

### [НИЗКАЯ] SEC-16 — Duplicated client-IP extraction and two parallel moderation-profile services

**Статус: Исправлено.** Обе половины. Дубль извлечения IP снят одним GetClientAddress (2107db2b); вторая служба модераторского профиля ModerationProfileApiService удалена вместе с сидером, в Features/Moderation/Profiles осталась одна реализация

`WebAuthenticationService.cs:63-76` and `AuthenticationApiService.cs:156-169` are byte-identical `ExtractClientIp` implementations, comment included. Separately, `Features/Moderation/Profiles/` contains both `ModeratedProfileApiService.cs` and `ModerationProfileApiService.cs`, each independently deciding PII visibility (`ModeratedProfileApiService.cs:78-92,122-124` vs `ModerationProfileApiService.cs:84,104,121,134`).

Последствие: SEC-08 has to be fixed in two places and either copy can be missed. More seriously, two independent implementations of "who may see an email address and an IP" is an SSOT violation on a privacy decision — the two will drift, and the drift will not be caught by any test.

Исправление: Delete both `ExtractClientIp` copies in favour of forwarded-headers middleware. Collapse the two moderation-profile services into one, or extract the PII-visibility predicate into a single shared policy object that both call.

### [НИЗКАЯ] SEC-17 — PasswordHashVersion is persisted but never read — no rehash-on-login upgrade path exists

**Статус: Частично.** Закрыта та половина, которая была настоящей ловушкой: смена пароля обновляла хеш и соль, но не версию, то есть строка получала версию, которая больше не описывает ее хеш - безвредно при одной схеме и невосстановимо при двух. Версия теперь пишется вместе с хешем, а сама четверка перестала быть литералом в пяти местах и имеет одного владельца. Диспетчер версий не написан намеренно: легаси-схемы в коде нет вообще, поэтому это был бы код без единой исполняемой ветки; его место - вместе с конвертером DM2

`AuthenticatedUser` carries `PasswordHashVersion` (visible in `test/DM.Web.API.IntegrationTests/CustomWebApplicationFactory.cs:286`), and the entity persists it, but `SecurityManager.ComparePasswords` (`:30-37`) and `HashProvider.ComputeHash` (`:31-43`) branch on nothing — the Argon2id parameters are compile-time constants with no version dispatch.

Последствие: The field implies a migration capability that does not exist. When the OWASP parameters change (they move roughly every two years), there is no mechanism to upgrade stored hashes on successful login, and no way to distinguish upgraded from legacy rows. The cost of adding it later, with the field already occupied by a meaningless value, is higher than adding it now.

Исправление: Either use the field — dispatch `ComputeHash` on the stored version and transparently rehash with current parameters inside the successful-comparison branch — or drop it so it does not read as an implemented control.


---

# Композиционный корень, конфигурация, hosted-сервисы, наблюдаемость — C-

Individually the pieces are competently built (the Autofac shadowing fix plus its regression test, PeriodDigestService's idempotency design, the liveness/readiness split), but the composition root as a whole is unverified wiring: endpoint rate-limit policies never execute and six controllers reference a policy that does not exist, the documented production deploy cannot build and publishes unauthenticated datastores to the internet, an unfinished refactor left three live/dead class pairs in the API assembly, and the observability stack is largely decorative.

## Что сделано хорошо

- The Autofac-shadowing fix is done properly, not patched. `ModuleRegistrationExtensions.cs:108` adds `PreserveExistingDefaults()` with a comment that names the exact mechanism (modules apply after `Populate`), and `test/DM.Web.API.Tests/Features/General/DbContextScopeResolutionShould.cs` reconstructs the real wiring shape (`Populate` then `RegisterModule<PersistenceModule>`) and asserts four distinct properties including `context.ContextId.Lease.Should().BePositive()` — that last assertion is what actually proves the pool is engaged rather than bypassed. That is a better regression test than most teams write.
- The blanket scan has deliberate hygiene, not just a wildcard: `ModuleRegistrationExtensions.cs:90-99` excludes abstract types, types with non-public constructors, `Exception` subclasses, and `IHostedService` implementations. The `IHostedService` exclusion in particular prevents every background service from being double-instantiated by the scan, and `CoreModule.cs:72` documents why `StorageBucketInitializer` is registered through `AddHostedService` instead.
- `HostedServices/PeriodDigestService.cs` is genuinely production-grade concurrency work and the only hosted service written for more than one replica: a marker table plus partial unique index for idempotency (`:135-137`), compensating delete of the orphaned topic when the marker fails to land (`:186-197`), backdating so a catch-up run lands in the correct historical position (`:148-150`), a set-based `ExecuteUpdateAsync` to recompute the board's denormalized last-topic fields instead of trusting the change tracker (`:215-223`), and an explicit written rationale for choosing compensation over a wrapping transaction given `EnableRetryOnFailure`.
- Health checks are split with the correct semantics rather than one catch-all endpoint: `Startup.cs:443` liveness uses `Predicate = _ => false` so a sick dependency never kills the container, `:450` readiness filters on the `ready` tag, `:457` exposes full detail separately. The tag assignment at `:178-190` is consistent across all three probes.
- Logs and traces are actually correlated, which is the part teams usually skip: `ActivityEnricher.cs` and `CorrelationMiddleware.cs:42-49` push `TraceId`/`SpanId` into the Serilog `LogContext`, so an OpenSearch log line can be pivoted to the matching Jaeger span. The correlation token is also client-supplyable via `X-Dm-Correlation-Token` for support workflows.
- Migration mode is cleanly gated rather than bolted on: `Startup.cs:153-171` suppresses all eleven hosted services when `MigrateOnStart` is set, `:372` threads the same flag into `MessageQueuingModule` to disable the outbox processor, and `:167-169` explains why `StorageBucketInitializer` specifically must not run in the migration container (no S3 access there). `docker-compose.yml:418-435` matches with `restart: "no"` and `service_completed_successfully`.
- The rate limiter's *design* is well thought through even though its wiring is broken: partition keys fall back user → IP → anon (`Startup.cs:259-261`), the upload and search policies pick the right window shapes (fixed vs sliding), and `OnRejected` at `:305-327` emits a proper `application/problem+json` body with a `Retry-After` header derived from lease metadata instead of a bare 429.
- EF Core is configured with resilience defaults that most projects forget: `Startup.cs:129-133` sets `EnableRetryOnFailure(3, 5s)` and a 30s command timeout on top of `AddDbContextPool`.

## Подтвержденные находки

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] CR-01 — Every endpoint-specific rate-limit policy is dead — UseRateLimiter runs before UseRouting, and the "default" policy is never registered

**Статус: Исправлено.** То же, что API-01 (304e618a)

**Доказательство.** `src/DM.Web.API/Startup.cs:431-432` calls `.UseRateLimiter()` and only then `.UseRouting()`. `RateLimitingMiddleware` reads `HttpContext.GetEndpoint()` to find `EnableRateLimitingAttribute` metadata; before `UseRouting` the endpoint is always null, so only `options.GlobalLimiter` (100 req/min per IP, `Startup.cs:210-218`) ever applies. Meanwhile 18 endpoints carry `[EnableRateLimiting(...)]` — `Features/Account/Authentication/AuthenticationController.cs:25` ("auth", 5/min), `Features/Account/Recovery/RecoveryController.cs:28`, `Features/Account/Credentials/CredentialsController.cs:28`, `Features/General/Upload/UploadController.cs:127` ("uploads", 10/min), `Features/Search/MessageSearchController.cs:64` ("sliding"). Separately, six controllers reference a policy name that is never registered anywhere: `Features/Personal/Profiles/ProfileController.cs:23`, `Features/Personal/Blacklists/Blac

**Последствие.** Login, password-recovery and credential-change endpoints have no brute-force protection beyond the generic 100 req/min-per-IP global limit — a 20x weaker ceiling than the 5/min the code believes it enforces, and trivially defeated from a handful of IPs. Uploads have no 10/min anti-flood. Worse, the two defects mask each other: the moment someone applies the documented middleware-order fix, the six "default" controllers start resolving an unregistered policy at request time, turning a security fix into an outage on the profile/subscription/blacklist surface. `appsettings.json` ships `RateLimiting.Enabled=false` and integration tests run with the no-op limiters, so nothing in CI can catch either half.

**Исправление.** Move `.UseRateLimiter()` to after `.UseRouting()` in `Startup.cs`, and in the same change either register a `"default"` policy in both the enabled and disabled branches of `AddRateLimiter` or remove the six `[EnableRateLimiting("default")]` attributes. Then add an integration test with `RateLimiting:Enabled=true` that posts 6 logins and asserts the 6th returns 429 with a `Retry-After` header — without that test this regresses silently again.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Both halves reproduced empirically, not just read. I built a throwaway ASP.NET Core 8 app on the same shared framework the project runs (Microsoft.AspNetCore.App 8.0.22) with a 1-req/min named policy plus an endpoint referencing an unregistered policy. With UseRateLimiter() before UseRouting() the 1/min endpoint returned 200,200,200 (policy inert); with the order swapped it returned 200,429,429. The unregistered-policy endpoint threw "System.InvalidOperationException: This endpoint requires a rate limiting policy with name nope, but no such policy exists." at Microsoft.AspNetCore.RateLimiting.RateLimitingMiddleware.<CreateEndpointLimiter>b__15_0 and returned HTTP 500 on every request. src/DM.Web.API/Startup.cs:431-432 is exactly that broken order, and src/DM.Web.API/Program.cs:36-39 uses Host.CreateDefaultBuilder(...).ConfigureWebHostDefaults(w => w.UseDefault<Startup>()) -> classic UseS

### [КРИТИЧНО] CR-02 — The production compose publishes every datastore on 0.0.0.0, including an unauthenticated MongoDB, and the setup script's firewall rules cannot constrain them

**Статус: Исправлено.** Все хранилища на 127.0.0.1, Mongo с аутентификацией и пользователем приложения с readWrite, служебные интерфейсы (Prometheus, Grafana, дашборды, порты воркеров) тоже уведены на loopback. Наружу публикуется единственный порт API. Замечание находки про брандмауэр учтено: правила на INPUT докерский порт не ловят, поэтому граница - адрес привязки (6b5fa1de, f9a3e8e2, 5f5ede96)

**Доказательство.** `docker/setup-server.sh:46` runs `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.preview.yml up -d --build` as the production bootstrap — the same `docker-compose.yml` used for local dev. That file publishes to the host: Postgres `:93 '5432:5432'`, Mongo `:118 '27017:27017'`, OpenSearch `:141 '9200:9200'`, RabbitMQ `:198-199 '15672:15672'`/`'5672:5672'`, MinIO `:244-245`, Grafana `:356 '3000:3000'`, Prometheus `:336 '9090:9090'`, and the API `:535 '5000:5000'`. The Mongo service (`:114-135`) sets no `MONGO_INITDB_ROOT_USERNAME`/`PASSWORD` and `DM_ConnectionStrings__Mongo` (`:21`) carries no credentials. `docker-compose.preview.yml` overrides nothing about ports — it only adds nginx and sets `DM_CdnConfiguration__PublicUrl`. The only firewalling is `setup-server.sh:22-23`, two `iptables -I INPUT ... --dport 80/443 -j ACCEPT` rules; Docker's published ports are DNAT'd in `nat/PRERO

**Последствие.** A server provisioned by the documented one-liner exposes an authentication-free MongoDB holding user documents and unread counters to the public internet, plus the Postgres port, the RabbitMQ management UI, MinIO's console, and Grafana. This is full data loss/exfiltration exposure, not a hardening nit. Secondarily it makes the API's `/metrics` and `/_health/detail` (`Startup.cs:440,457` — the latter serialises per-dependency exception messages via `UIResponseWriter`) reachable off-host even though nginx only proxies `/v1/` and `/notifications` (`docker/nginx/nginx.conf:69,81`).

**Исправление.** Split the compose file: keep `docker-compose.yml` as the dev file and add a production overlay that binds every infra port to `127.0.0.1:` (or drops the `ports:` mapping entirely, since all inter-service traffic already goes over the `dm-full-app` network by container name). Give Mongo root credentials and put them in the connection string. Have `setup-server.sh` set the `DOCKER-USER` chain policy rather than `INPUT`.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Every citation checks out. docker-compose.yml publishes with bare host mappings (=0.0.0.0): Postgres :93, Mongo :118, OpenSearch :141, RabbitMQ :198-199, MinIO :244-245, Prometheus :336, Grafana :356, API :535. The mongo service (:114-135) sets no MONGO_INITDB_ROOT_USERNAME/PASSWORD, so mongod runs with access control disabled, and no Mongo credentials exist anywhere in the repo (DM_ConnectionStrings__Mongo :22 and every appsettings*.json use credential-free mongodb:// URIs). docker-compose.preview.yml changes only nginx/dmfront/CdnPublicUrl (:38) and no ports. setup-server.sh:45 (finding said :46 — trivial off-by-one) runs both files; :22-23 are additive INPUT ACCEPTs, and Docker's published ports are DNAT'd through nat/PREROUTING into the FORWARD/DOCKER chains, never traversing INPUT, so they neither open nor close anything.

I could not refute it on any of the usual grounds. (1) Guard

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] CR-03 — The production bootstrap cannot build, and systemd restarts a different topology than the installer deployed

**Статус: Исправлено.** dm3.service поднимал базовый compose без оверлея: systemd после перезагрузки заменял сайт голым API, а ExecStop гасил все контейнеры проекта. Обе строки получили список файлов инсталлятора, и расхождение теперь ловит гейт в CI. Сборка боевого стенда чинится тем же контекстом SPA (5ed790e1)

**Доказательство.** `docker/docker-compose.preview.yml:42-45` builds `dmfront` from `context: ../frontend/DM.Web.Modern` — that path does not exist; the client lives at `src/DM.Web.Client` (confirmed by `.github/workflows/dotnet.yml:46,53,80`). So `setup-server.sh:46`, which runs with `--build`, fails on the frontend service. Independently, `docker/dm3.service` (the unit that `setup-server.sh:37` enables for autostart) has `ExecStart=/usr/bin/docker compose up -d` with no `-f docker-compose.preview.yml` — while `ExecStop` runs `docker compose down`, which tears down every container in the `dm` project including nginx and dmfront.

**Последствие.** The documented single-command production install cannot complete. And even if it is worked around manually, the first reboot or `systemctl restart dm3` brings the stack back up *without* nginx and *without* the frontend, so port 80 is dead and the site is down until someone re-runs the two-file compose command by hand. `watchtower` is also profile-gated to `production`/`mirror` (`docker-compose.yml:571-573`) and neither the installer nor the unit passes `--profile`, so the auto-update mechanism labelled onto `dmapi` (`:528-529`) never runs either.

**Исправление.** Point the `dmfront` build context at `../src/DM.Web.Client`, add `-f /opt/dm3/docker/docker-compose.yml -f /opt/dm3/docker/docker-compose.preview.yml --profile production` to both `ExecStart` and `ExecStop` in `dm3.service`, and add a CI job that runs `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.preview.yml config --quiet` plus a build of the frontend context so path rot is caught.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Refutation failed on every front. (1) docker/docker-compose.preview.yml:43-45 really does set context: ../frontend/DM.Web.Modern, and that directory does not exist — git log --diff-filter=D shows frontend/DM.Web.Modern/Dockerfile was deleted in 9fa0535c ("refactor: restructure project to Clean Architecture naming"), the same commit that added src/DM.Web.Client/Dockerfile. docs/guides/DEPLOYMENT.md:36 itself names src/DM.Web.Client/Dockerfile as the frontend Dockerfile, so the compose path is rot, not an intentional alternate layout. (2) No escape hatch exists: dmfront is defined ONLY in the preview override (no dmfront in docker-compose.yml), its tag dm-front:local is never published (DEPLOYMENT.md:28 lists only 4 CI images: dm-api + 3 consumers), and setup-server.sh:45 runs `up -d --build` under `set -euo pipefail` (:2) — Compose builds all buildable services before creating any contain

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] CR-04 — An unfinished folder refactor left three live/dead class pairs inside the API assembly; the winning WarmupService has already lost functionality and the two realtime processors disagree on behaviour

**Статус: Исправлено.** Мертвые Notifications/RealtimeNotificationConsumer и Processor удалены; они разошлись с живыми копиями (c91d8d4d)

**Доказательство.** `src/DM.Web.API/Warmup/WarmupService.cs` and `src/DM.Web.API/HostedServices/WarmupService.cs` are both compiled. `Startup.cs:156` writes the unqualified `AddHostedService<WarmupService>()` with only `using DM.Web.API.HostedServices;` (`Startup.cs:32`) in scope, so the **HostedServices** copy wins — and that copy is missing Phase 2, `WarmupPopularityScores` (`Warmup/WarmupService.cs:64-65,148-227`). `HostedServices/PopularityScoreService.cs:41,45-51` explicitly skips its own first run with the comment "Initial calculation is done by WarmupService" and awaits the timer before the first update. Same pattern for `Realtime/RealtimeNotificationProcessor.cs` vs `Notifications/RealtimeNotificationProcessor.cs`: both are `IProcessor<string, RealtimeNotification>`, both are picked up by the blanket `AsImplementedInterfaces()` scan (`Startup.cs:360`), and they diverge — the Realtime copy branches o

**Последствие.** Popularity scores are never computed at startup, so a fresh deploy or reseed serves zero-scored games and blogs for up to an hour (`PopularityScoreService.cs:28`) — and a maintainer fixing that by editing `Warmup/WarmupService.cs` will change nothing at all, because that file is dead. The realtime pair is worse: two registrations of the same closed generic interface exist in the container, and only the fact that `Realtime/RealtimeNotificationConsumer.cs:41` resolves the processor by *concrete type* (`BuildRabbit<RealtimeNotification, RealtimeNotificationProcessor>`) keeps the right one in play. Any refactor toward interface-based resolution, or an `IEnumerable<IProcessor<...>>` injection, silently picks a non-deterministic winner and guests stop receiving global chat.

**Исправление.** Delete `src/DM.Web.API/Warmup/`, `src/DM.Web.API/Notifications/RealtimeNotification{Consumer,Processor}.cs` and `src/DM.Web.API/Shared/BackgroundServices/` after porting `WarmupPopularityScores` into the surviving `HostedServices/WarmupService.cs`. Then add a container smoke test that asserts exactly one registration exists for `IProcessor<string, RealtimeNotification>` and for `IHostedService` implementations named `*WarmupService` — the blanket scan makes duplicate-type collisions invisible otherwise.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Every code citation verifies. Both WarmupService classes compile (DM.Web.API.csproj has no Compile Remove); Startup.cs:156 has only `using DM.Web.API.HostedServices;` (:32) and no `using DM.Web.API.Warmup;`, so simple-name lookup unambiguously binds the HostedServices copy, which stops after Phase 1 (HostedServices/WarmupService.cs:60-63) — WarmupPopularityScores exists solely in the dead file (Warmup/WarmupService.cs:64-65,148-227). PopularityScoreService.cs:41,45-50 really does skip its first run ("Initial calculation done by WarmupService") and awaits a 1-hour tick (:28), so that contract is broken; `git log -S WarmupPopularityScores` shows it was added in c9977c46, the same commit that created PopularityScoreService.cs, meaning it has never executed. The realtime divergence is real (Realtime/RealtimeNotificationProcessor.cs:30-38 broadcasts NewGlobalChatMessage to Clients.All vs Noti

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] CR-07 — RealtimeNotificationConsumer throws synchronously out of ExecuteAsync — a RabbitMQ outage at startup crash-loops the whole API

**Статус: Исправлено.** Yield + catch, отказ Rabbit больше не роняет API (24a43d5e)

**Доказательство.** `src/DM.Web.API/Realtime/RealtimeNotificationConsumer.cs:31` declares `protected override Task ExecuteAsync(...)` as a non-async method, and `:42` calls `_consumeRetryPolicy.Execute(consumer.Subscribe)` — a synchronous Polly `WaitAndRetry(5, attempt => TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1 << attempt))` (`:26-28`), i.e. 2+4+8+16+32 = 62 seconds of retries. Because there is no `await` before it, the whole body runs inline inside `BackgroundService.StartAsync`, so an exhausted retry throws straight out of `IHostedService.StartAsync` and aborts host startup. Every other hosted service in the solution swallows non-cancellation exceptions in its loop (e.g. `TokenCleanupService.cs:53-57`, `PeriodDigestService.cs:89-100`); this one is the sole exception. The queue is also declared with `Exclusive = true` on a fixed name `dm.notifications.api` (`:35-40`).

**Последствие.** If RabbitMQ is unreachable for more than ~62 seconds while the API starts — a broker restart, a watchtower-triggered image pull that lands during Rabbit maintenance, a slow node — the API process exits, `restart: unless-stopped` (`docker-compose.yml:557`) brings it back, and it crash-loops until the broker recovers. `depends_on: rabbitmq: service_healthy` (`:433-434`) only covers the cold-start case, not restarts. The `Exclusive = true` fixed-name queue is also a hard architectural ceiling: a second API replica's `Subscribe` gets `RESOURCE_LOCKED` and, by the same path, kills itself on startup.

**Исправление.** Make `ExecuteAsync` async and move the subscribe into an indefinite retry loop with capped backoff that logs and keeps trying rather than giving up after five attempts, so a broker outage degrades realtime notifications instead of taking the HTTP API down. Replace the exclusive fixed-name queue with a per-instance queue name (or an auto-delete non-exclusive queue bound to the same exchange) before any second replica is considered.

**Проверка оппонентом.** MECHANISM VERIFIED — every code citation about the defect itself is accurate. `src/DM.Web.API/Realtime/RealtimeNotificationConsumer.cs:31` is `protected override Task ExecuteAsync(...)` (non-async, `return Task.CompletedTask` at :46); `:42` is `_consumeRetryPolicy.Execute(consumer.Subscribe)` under the policy at `:26-28` (`WaitAndRetry(5, attempt => TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1 << attempt))` = 2+4+8+16+32 = 62 s, then rethrow); `:35-40` sets `Exclusive = true` on fixed queue `dm.notifications.api`. Since the override is not `async`, a throw propagates synchronously out of `BackgroundService.StartAsync` (which calls `ExecuteAsync` inline and returns the completed task), aborting `IHost.StartAsync`. `src/DM.Web.API/Program.cs:23-26` is `CreateWebHostBuilder(args).WithDmConfiguration().Build().Run()` — no try/catch — so the process exits non-zero. The service is registered unconditionally in prod

## Опровергнуто оппонентом (в работу не берем)

- **CR-05** — RegisterMapper still shadows MS.DI the same way RegisterDefaultTypes did — AutoMapper's AllowNullCollections is silently discarded. Причина отклонения: The mechanical half of CR-05 is accurate, but the impact, the severity, and the prescribed fix are all wrong — and the fix would take the API down.

VERIFIED AS STATED (the only part that holds): `src/DM.Web.API/Startup.cs:110` does call `.AddAutoMapper(config => config.AllowNullCollections = true)`, and `src/DM.Infrastructure.Core/Extensions/ModuleRegistrationExtensions.cs:133-146` does re-regist
- **CR-06** — OutboxEvents has no index and no retention — the outbox poller degrades into a full table scan every 5 seconds, forever. Причина отклонения: The schema facts are accurate but the premise that makes them a "high" is false: nothing in the repository ever inserts an OutboxEvent, so the table is permanently empty and cannot degrade.

VERIFIED AS STATED (the harmless half): `src/DM.Infrastructure.Persistence/Migrations/20260516025527_InitialCreate.cs:97-115` does create `OutboxEvents` with only `table.PrimaryKey("PK_OutboxEvents", x => x.Id

## Средние и низкие

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] CR-08 — Configuration binding is hand-copied across four hosts and has already diverged — CoreModule's OpenSearch client is unbound in three of them

**Статус: Частично.** Названный в находке симптом снят: несвязанного клиента OpenSearch больше нет ни в одном хосте - вместе с движком ушли и регистрация в CoreModule, и секция SearchEngineConfiguration. Сама причина осталась: биндинг конфигурации по-прежнему переписывается руками в каждом хосте, теперь в трех

`CoreModule.cs:57-66` registers `ConnectionSettings` from `IOptions<SearchEngineConfiguration>` and does `new Uri(configuration.Endpoint)`. `SearchEngineConfiguration.Endpoint` defaults to `string.Empty` (`DM.Domain.Core/Search/SearchEngineConfiguration.cs:16`). That section is bound in exactly one place — `DM.Workers.SearchIndexer/Startup.cs:49` — yet `CoreModule` is registered by all four hosts: `DM.Web.API/Startup.cs:376`, `DM.Workers.Mail/Startup.cs:67`, `DM.Workers.NotificationDispatcher/Startup.cs:79`, `DM.Workers.SearchIndexer/Startup.cs:75`. The API binds sixteen sections at `Startup.cs:72-87` and `SearchEngineConfiguration` is not among them, even though `docker-compose.yml:35-37` faithfully supplies `DM_SearchEngineConfiguration__*` to every workload. The same shape applies to `CdnConfiguration`, which `MinioS3ClientProvider.cs:24` needs for `CanBeUsed()` and which the Mail and NotificationDispatcher hosts never bind. There is no shared `AddDmConfiguration()` extension — each host retypes an overlapping subset by hand.

Последствие: `ISearchEngineRepository`/`ISearchService` are registered but unresolvable in three of four hosts: the first consumer to inject one gets a `UriFormatException` from `new Uri("")` at resolve time, not at startup. Likewise `IAmazonS3` resolution in the mail/notification workers would hit `.First(p => p.CanBeUsed())` (`CoreModule.cs:46-50`) with no matching provider and throw `Sequence contains no matching element`. These are latent because nothing currently injects them — the next person to add a search endpoint to the API discovers it in production. Architecturally the bug is that a module declares its config dependency but the binding lives in every host's Startup, so the two drift by construction.

Исправление: Move each module's `Configure<T>` calls into the module that consumes them (an `IServiceCollection` extension shipped next to `CoreModule`, `PersistenceModule`, `MessageQueuingModule`), so registering the module cannot leave its options unbound. Add `.ValidateOnStart()` on `SearchEngineConfiguration`, `CdnConfiguration` and `CryptoConfiguration` so a missing value fails the host rather than a request.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] CR-09 — The observability stack is largely ceremony: scrape targets point at hostnames that do not exist, alert rules reference exporters that never start, and there is no alertmanager

**Статус: Частично.** Таргеты, алерты и дашборд приведены к реальности; ложных алертов нет. Трассировка и логи не пересматривались (a0250f1f)

`docker/prometheus.yml:12,17,22` scrapes `dm-notifications-consumer:5000`, `dm-search-engine-consumer:5000`, `dm-email-sender-consumer:5000` — the actual container names in `docker-compose.yml` are `dm-notification-worker` (`:495`), `dm-search-worker` (`:467`), `dm-mail-worker` (`:439`). Those three jobs can never resolve. `docker/prometheus/alerts.yml` then defines `ConsumerDown` on `up{job=~"dm-(notifications|search-engine|email-sender)-consumer"} == 0`, so it fires permanently. Conversely `PostgresDown` uses `pg_up` and `HighMemoryUsage` uses `node_memory_*`, but `postgres-exporter` and `node-exporter` are profile-gated to `monitoring`/`full` (`docker-compose.yml:390-392,412-414`) and neither `setup-server.sh:46` nor `dm3.service` passes a profile — those series never exist, so the rules are permanently silent. `prometheus.yml` has no `alerting:` block and no alertmanager service exists anywhere; `docs/guides/MONITORING.md:158` references a `docker/prometheus/alertmanager.yml` that is not in the repo. `docker/opensearch/ism-policy.json` (30-day log retention) is referenced by nothing — not mounted, not applied by any script — while `MONITORING.md:194` still lists retention as a to-do.

Последствие: Three of five critical alert rules are structurally incapable of firing and one fires 24/7, which trains whoever is watching to ignore the channel — and nothing routes any of them anywhere regardless. Log indices grow without bound because the ISM policy was written but never installed. The net effect is a stack that looks instrumented in review and provides no actionable signal in an incident.

Исправление: Rename the three Prometheus jobs to the real container names (or rename the containers), add an `alerting:` block plus an alertmanager service with at least one receiver, move `postgres-exporter`/`node-exporter` out of the profile gate or drop the rules that depend on them, and apply `ism-policy.json` from an init step so log retention is real. A single CI check that asserts every `job=` referenced in `alerts.yml` exists in `prometheus.yml` and every metric prefix has a running exporter would have caught all of this.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] CR-10 — Serilog runs at Debug in every environment and user emails are logged at Information into a log store with no applied retention

**Статус: Исправлено.** Уровень логирования стал зависеть от окружения, шум фреймворка режется override'ами по источнику - одно правило на оба приемника вместо фильтра на одном. Текст SQL уходит в трассу только в разработке. Адреса почты убраны из шаблонов во всех 12 местах: адрес называет человека, а у хранилища нет retention; пофамильный след - журнал безопасности. Уточнение к находке: заявление про интернет-доступность хранилища устарело, порты на loopback (c0d9d638)

`LoggingConfiguration.cs:31-48` hardcodes `.MinimumLevel.Debug()` with no environment branch and no per-source overrides; the OpenSearch sink only excludes `Microsoft`-sourced events (`:38`), while the Console sink (`:46-47`) receives everything including framework Debug. `DM.Domain.Account/Features/Authentication/AuthenticationService.cs:134` logs `"User authenticated successfully. UserId={UserId}, Email={Email}"` on every login and `:68` logs `Email={Email}` on pending-registration login attempts — both at Information, both shipped to OpenSearch. As established in CR-09, the 30-day ISM policy is never applied.

Последствие: Every user's email address is indexed in an unbounded, internet-reachable (CR-02) log store, joined to `UserId`, `TraceId` and request context — that is a personal-data retention problem, not just noise. Separately, Debug-level output across all of Serilog plus `SetDbStatementForText = true` on the EF instrumentation (`LoggingConfiguration.cs:56`, full SQL text into every trace) makes log/trace volume and cost scale with traffic rather than with incidents, and buries the `LogWarning`-level signals the app does emit.

Исправление: Set the minimum level from `ASPNETCORE_ENVIRONMENT` (Information in production, Debug in development) with `MinimumLevel.Override` for the chatty namespaces, drop `{Email}` from the authentication log messages (the `UserId` already identifies the principal), and gate `SetDbStatementForText` on non-production. Apply the ISM policy.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] CR-11 — No leader election or locking on eleven hosted services, and the deployment is pinned to a single instance by container_name — the scaling ceiling is structural

**Статус: Открыто.** 

`Startup.cs:155-170` registers eleven hosted services plus `OutboxProcessor` via `MessageQueuingModule.cs:51-53`. Of these, only `PeriodDigestService` is written for concurrent instances (marker + unique index + compensation, `:135-197`). `PendencyReminderService.cs:94-118` reads stale pendencies, stamps `LastReminderUtc` and emits events with no locking — two instances double-send every reminder. `UploadOrphanCleanupService.cs:88-141` takes a 200-row batch and deletes S3 objects with no claim. `OutboxProcessor.cs:70-92` selects and marks with no `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` — two instances publish every event twice. There is no `IDistributedLock`, no advisory-lock helper, no leader election anywhere in the solution. Meanwhile every compose service pins `container_name` (`docker-compose.yml:526` `'dm-api'`), which makes `docker compose up --scale` impossible, and `RealtimeNotificationConsumer.cs:39` declares an exclusive fixed-name queue (see CR-07).

Последствие: Not a bug today — it is an architectural dead-end. The system cannot be scaled horizontally or deployed with zero downtime (a rolling deploy briefly runs two instances, which is enough to double-send reminders and duplicate outbox publishes), and the mirror topology described in `MirrorConfiguration` (`appsettings.json`) makes a second live instance a plausible near-term requirement. The cost of retrofitting grows with every new hosted service written against the single-instance assumption.

Исправление: Pick one mechanism now and apply it uniformly: a Postgres advisory lock (`pg_try_advisory_lock`) acquired per service name at the top of each tick is enough and needs no new infrastructure. Wrap it in a shared `SingletonBackgroundService` base class so the assumption is enforced by the type rather than by each author remembering. Remove `container_name` pins from the workload services.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] CR-12 — Startup is a 519-line god class mixing policy bodies, reflection-by-string and pipeline order

**Статус: Открыто.** 

`Startup.cs:66-352` is a single 286-line `ConfigureServices` that inlines sixteen `Configure<T>` calls, six rate-limiter policy bodies with duplicated partition-key expressions (`:221-300`), the compression/caching/Kestrel/form limits, the health-check wiring, the gRPC client and the SignalR/Swagger/MVC setup. `RegisterDomainServices` (`:468-518`) reaches internal domain types through string literals — `accountAssembly.GetType("DM.Domain.Account.Features.Identity.IdentityProvider")!` (`:496-498`) and `moderationAssembly.GetType("DM.Domain.Moderation.Authorization.ModerationIntentionResolver")!` (`:514`) — with a null-forgiving `!` on each, and the method's own comment says it "replaces individual Module.cs files in Domain.* projects". `Startup.cs:338-339` also constructs `HttpContextAccessor` and `BbParserProvider` by hand into fields so they can be re-registered in `ConfigureContainer` (`:363-368`).

Последствие: Renaming an internal class in a domain project compiles clean and then throws `NullReferenceException` during container build at startup — the type system has been opted out of at the exact place where failures are most expensive. The centralisation also inverts the modular-monolith boundary the rest of the codebase maintains: `DM.Web.API` now owns lifetime decisions for `DM.Domain.Account` internals, so a domain module can no longer state its own composition. And the file's size makes middleware ordering (CR-01) easy to get wrong and hard to review.

Исправление: Give each domain project back a `Module` class that registers its own internals with normal `typeof(...)` references and `InternalsVisibleTo` where needed — that removes all four string literals and the null-forgiving operators. Extract the rate-limiter configuration into `RateLimitingExtensions.AddDmRateLimiting(configuration)` with one shared partition-key helper, and the health checks into `AddDmHealthChecks`. Target a `ConfigureServices` that is a list of extension-method calls.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] CR-13 — RegisterDefaultTypes and RegisterMapper depend on Assembly.GetCallingAssembly() without NoInlining

**Статус: Опровергнуто.** NoInlining не нужен: Assembly.GetCallingAssembly помечен RequireSecObject в CoreLib .NET 8, что запрещает рантайму встраивать вызывающего. Предсказанный молчаливый отказ недостижим, номера строк в доказательстве устарели

`ModuleRegistrationExtensions.cs:77-78` is `public static ContainerBuilder RegisterDefaultTypes(this ContainerBuilder builder) => builder.RegisterDefaultTypes(Assembly.GetCallingAssembly());` and `:118-119` is the identical shape for `RegisterMapper`. Neither carries `[MethodImpl(MethodImplOptions.NoInlining)]`. These one-line forwarders are prime inlining candidates in Release, and `GetCallingAssembly` returns the caller of the frame it is executed in. The pattern is relied on at `CoreModule.cs:28`, `MessageQueuingModule.cs:56`, `Startup.cs:360-361`, `DM.Workers.Mail/Startup.cs:65`, `DM.Workers.SearchIndexer/Startup.cs:72`, `DM.Workers.NotificationDispatcher/Startup.cs:77`.

Последствие: If the JIT inlines the forwarder into `CoreModule.Load`, `GetCallingAssembly` returns Autofac's assembly (the caller of `Load`) instead of `DM.Infrastructure.Core`, and the entire module registers nothing — every service in that assembly silently disappears from the container, surfacing as an unrelated `ComponentNotRegisteredException` deep in a request path. It is a Release-only, tiering-dependent failure, which is the worst possible failure shape: it will not reproduce under a debugger.

Исправление: Annotate both parameterless overloads with `[MethodImpl(MethodImplOptions.NoInlining)]`, or better, delete them and require the explicit `RegisterDefaultTypes(typeof(SomeMarker).Assembly)` form everywhere — the explicit call sites already dominate (`Startup.cs:490-491`) and the implicit one saves nothing.

### [СРЕДНЯЯ] CR-14 — appsettings.json is committed with secrets and RateLimiting disabled, contradicting the template that sits next to it

**Статус: Исправлено.** Слепое игнорирование **/appsettings.json заменено конвенцией: отслеживаемый файл несет только localhost-дефолты, секреты приходят из DM_* и secrets/. Удален неотслеживаемый appsettings.template.json. Заявление находки про отключенный RateLimiting неверно - он true и в файле, и в дефолте кода (f9a3e8e2)

`src/DM.Web.API/appsettings.template.json:3` states `"_security_note": "NEVER commit appsettings.json to git - it contains secrets!"` — and `git ls-files` shows `src/DM.Web.API/appsettings.json` is tracked. The committed file carries `CryptoConfiguration.KeyBase64` (`QUJDREVGR0hJSktMTU5PUFFSU1RVVldYWVphYmNkZWY=`), RabbitMQ and MinIO credentials, and `"RateLimiting": { "Enabled": false }` as the very first key. The template has drifted too: it omits `ImageProxyConfiguration`, `ProbationConfiguration`, `OutboxConfiguration` and `MirrorConfiguration` (all bound at `Startup.cs:76,85-87`), and its `TracingEndpoint` placeholder is `http://<JAEGER_HOST>:1234` — not a real OTLP port.

Последствие: `appsettings.json` is the lowest-precedence layer for *all* environments, so `RateLimiting.Enabled=false` is the production default unless something overrides it — and nothing in `docker-compose.yml`'s `x-workload-env` sets `DM_RateLimiting__Enabled`. Combined with CR-01, rate limiting is off twice over. The committed crypto key also means the AES-GCM key used for whatever `CryptoConfiguration` protects is public in the repo history and must be treated as compromised. And a template that cannot produce a working config is a template nobody uses.

Исправление: Add `appsettings.json` to `.gitignore`, `git rm --cached` it, rotate the crypto key, and move the dev values into `appsettings.Development.json`. Invert the rate-limiting default to `Enabled: true` so the safe value is the fallback and tests opt out explicitly. Regenerate the template from the actual set of bound sections and add a CI check that every `Configure<T>` in a Startup has a matching template section.

### [НИЗКАЯ] CR-15 — Startup.Configure leases a pooled DbContext from the root container for the process lifetime and hard-exits without flushing logs

**Статус: Исправлено.** Контекст для миграции берется из scope, а не параметром Configure; аренда из пула больше не удерживается на весь процесс (29ea7740)

`Startup.cs:399-408` takes `DmDbContext dbContext` as a `Configure` parameter, which resolves from the root service provider, not a request scope — with `AddDbContextPool` (`:124`) that checks out a pooled context that is only returned when the root container is disposed. It exists solely for the `_migrateOnStart` branch. That branch then calls `Environment.Exit(0)` (`:407`) immediately after `Migrate()`. There is no `Log.CloseAndFlush()` anywhere in the solution, and no `HostOptions.ShutdownTimeout` or `BackgroundServiceExceptionBehavior` configuration either. The `ILogger<Startup> logger` parameter at `:402` is never used.

Последствие: `Environment.Exit(0)` terminates without running finalizers or disposing the Serilog sink, so the OpenSearch sink's in-flight batch is dropped — the migration container's logs, exactly the ones you want when a deploy migration misbehaves, are the least likely to arrive. The root-scope DbContext lease is one permanently checked-out pooled instance plus a violation of the scoped-from-root rule that the project has just spent effort enforcing elsewhere. The unused logger parameter is dead weight in a signature that already does too much.

Исправление: Move the migration branch into `Program.Main` as an explicit pre-host step that creates its own scope, disposes it, calls `Log.CloseAndFlush()` and returns an exit code instead of calling `Environment.Exit`. Drop `DmDbContext` and `ILogger<Startup>` from `Configure`. Add `Log.CloseAndFlush()` in a `finally` around `Build().Run()` in `Program.cs:24-26`.

### [НИЗКАЯ] CR-16 — The readiness probe is consumed by nothing, and the OTLP endpoint port is wrong outside Docker

**Статус: Исправлено.** Экспортер шлет OTLP по gRPC, а публиковался только порт HTTP-протокола; два воркера вообще были нацелены на легаси-эндпоинт Jaeger 14268/api/traces. Все четыре конфигурации приведены к одному протоколу и порту, порт опубликован (c0d9d638)

`Startup.cs:450-454` maps `/_ready` filtered on the `ready` tag (Postgres, Mongo, RabbitMQ). Nothing consumes it: `docker-compose.yml:454-459` health-checks `dmapi` against `/_health` (the liveness probe that is hardcoded to always report healthy via `Predicate = _ => false`), `docker-compose.preview.yml:20-21` gates nginx on `dmapi: condition: service_healthy` — i.e. on liveness — and there is no load balancer or orchestrator in the picture. Separately, `appsettings.json` sets `TracingEndpoint` to `http://localhost:4318` while `AddOtlpExporter` (`LoggingConfiguration.cs:60`) uses the OpenTelemetry .NET default protocol of gRPC, whose port is 4317 — which `docker-compose.yml:24` gets right and the template (`:1234`) gets nonsensically wrong.

Последствие: nginx starts proxying to the API the moment the process answers a socket, before Postgres/Mongo/Rabbit readiness is established, so the first requests after a deploy can 500 while the readiness endpoint that would have prevented it sits unused. Also: the health-check dependency set omits MinIO and OpenSearch, both of which the API depends on. Locally, traces are silently dropped because the exporter speaks gRPC at an HTTP/protobuf port — which makes the Jaeger datasource look broken to anyone debugging.

Исправление: Point the compose health check and the nginx `depends_on` gate at `/_ready`, keeping `/_health` for the container restart policy only. Add MinIO and OpenSearch checks with the `ready` tag (`AspNetCore.HealthChecks.Uris` is already referenced in the csproj). Fix the local `TracingEndpoint` to `:4317` and the template placeholder to a real port.


---

# Асинхронный обмен сообщениями и realtime — D

The client-side realtime layer is genuinely well-designed (push treated as a cache-invalidation hint with REST catch-up), but the server side is a facade: the transactional outbox has zero writers, 11 produced event types are bound to no queue so ~14 notification generators can never fire, consumers have no idempotency, and the whole API is single-instance-only while the deployment doc promises `replicas: 3`.

## Что сделано хорошо

- Realtime is architected as a hint, not a data channel — and that is the single decision that saves this dimension. `App.vue:206-230` responds to any unknown event by re-fetching the unread counter over REST instead of mutating local state, and `GlobalChatPage.vue:635-642` runs `watch(isSignalRConnected, () => fetchNewMessages())` with a 30s poll fallback that is a no-op while the socket is up. Missed messages self-heal, so the absence of a backplane and the at-most-once bus degrade into latency rather than data loss.
- The shared-socket singleton in `useSignalR.ts:118-255` is the most carefully reasoned code in the dimension. Lease-based ownership (`globalLeases`) so a page unmount cannot kill the App-owned connection; `negotiatedAsAuthenticated` tracked at negotiate time so a login/logout while another lease holds the socket forces a re-negotiation instead of leaking the previous user's stream; and `enqueueGlobalTransition` serializing connect/disconnect because App.vue and the chat page mount concurrently. Every one of those is a bug someone actually hit and fixed properly.
- The hub's security model is coherent and its invariant is stated where it can be checked. `NotificationHub.cs:14-27` documents why `[AllowAnonymous]` is safe (no client-invokable methods, no groups), and `UserConnectionService.Add` (`Realtime/UserConnectionService.cs:20-27`) enforces it by returning early for unauthenticated identities — so an anonymous connection is structurally incapable of being a per-user fan-out target. `OnDisconnectedAsync` deliberately removes by connection id rather than re-authenticating, with the reason (logout already invalidated the cookie) written down at `NotificationHub.cs:57-59`.
- Distributed tracing actually crosses the broker: `LoggingConfiguration.cs:59` calls `.AddJamqClientInstrumentation()`, so a produce/consume hop carries trace context into the workers rather than terminating at the API boundary. Most projects at this size never wire this.
- The notification fan-out avoids the obvious N+1. `NotificationEmailSender.cs:119-129` and `NotificationBotSender.cs:123-134` both bulk-load Mongo settings and the Postgres user rows once per event with `Filter.In`/`Contains`, then loop in memory. The email sender additionally wraps each recipient in its own try/catch (`NotificationEmailSender.cs:132-178`) so one bad address cannot abort the batch.
- `DM.Workers.Mail` is the one worker that is genuinely well-factored: a single `IProcessor` (`MailSendingProcessor.cs`), no database, no domain references, a lazily-opened SMTP connection, and a correlation id propagated into the MIME `Message-Id` (`MailSendingProcessor.cs:58`). It would survive being extracted or replaced.

## Подтвержденные находки

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] OUTBOX-DECORATIVE — The transactional outbox has zero writers — it is scaffolding, not a pattern

**Статус: Исправлено.** Каркас удален: сущность, таблица, процессор, конфигурация, тест; документация перестала обещать гарантию доставки (1876e028)

**Доказательство.** `OutboxEvent` (src/DM.Infrastructure.Persistence/Entities/Shared/OutboxEvent.cs), the `OutboxEvents` DbSet (DmDbContext.cs:723), the table (Migrations/20260516025527_InitialCreate.cs:97-115), `OutboxConfiguration`, `OutboxProcessor`, its DI registration (Web.API/Startup.cs:372) and four unit tests all exist. A repo-wide search for `new OutboxEvent` / `OutboxEvents.Add` across src/ and test/ returns nothing. Every one of the 81 publish sites bypasses it and calls RabbitMQ directly after the write has already committed — e.g. Forum/TopicCommentService.cs:89-91 (`await _repository.Create(...)` then `await _invokedEventProducer.SendAsync(EventType.NewTopicComment, ...)`). There is exactly one `BeginTransaction` in the entire codebase (Persistence/Repositories/Account/ActivationRepository.cs:72), so a same-transaction outbox write is not even structurally possible today. Meanwhile docs/archit

**Последствие.** There is no at-least-once guarantee anywhere. A broker blip, a pod restart, or an unhandled exception between the `SaveChanges` and the `SendAsync` permanently loses the event: no notification, no email, no search index entry, and no record that anything was lost. Worse, when `SendAsync` throws, the exception propagates out of the domain service to the controller — so the user gets HTTP 500 for a comment that was in fact created. The documented reliability property is false, and the four `OutboxProcessorShould` tests (which only assert that a BackgroundService starts and stops) make the gap invisible in CI.

**Исправление.** Pick one and commit to it. Either (a) finish the outbox: introduce a unit-of-work that appends an `OutboxEvent` row inside the same `SaveChanges` as the domain write, and delete the 81 direct `IEventProducer` call sites from the domain layer; or (b) delete the outbox scaffolding entirely, stop claiming reliability in SYSTEM.md/DATA_STORAGE.md, and wrap every `SendAsync` in a try/catch that logs and swallows so a broker outage cannot turn a successful write into a 500. Option (b) is honest and cheap; option (a) is what the docs already promise.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Attempted refutation failed on the core claim; it holds, but the severity and two evidence details do not.

VERIFIED AS STATED:
1. The scaffolding exists exactly as described: OutboxEvent entity (src/DM.Infrastructure.Persistence/Entities/Shared/OutboxEvent.cs:10-62), DbSet (src/DM.Infrastructure.Persistence/DmDbContext.cs:723), table (Migrations/20260516025527_InitialCreate.cs:97-115), OutboxConfiguration (src/DM.Infrastructure.Messaging/Outbox/OutboxConfiguration.cs), a fully functional OutboxProcessor with exponential backoff and dead-lettering (src/DM.Infrastructure.Messaging/Outbox/OutboxProcessor.cs:61-132), and live DI registration as IHostedService (src/DM.Infrastructure.Messaging/MessageQueuingModule.cs:49-54, wired from src/DM.Web.API/Startup.cs:372).
2. Zero writers — I searched harder than the original agent did. `new OutboxEvent`, `OutboxEvents.Add`, `Set<OutboxEvent>` all r

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] ROUTING-GAP — 11 published event types are bound to no queue — RabbitMQ silently discards them, killing 14 notification generators

**Статус: Исправлено.** Привязка выводится из генераторов; 41 -> 56 привязок, проверено в брокере (5400eba8)

**Доказательство.** `NotificationDispatcherConsumer.cs:41-107` enumerates 41 explicit routing keys for the `dm.notifications` queue. Diffing that list against every `EventType` referenced in `Implementation/Notifiers/**` yields 14 generators whose trigger event is not bound: AccountLocked, BanIssued, BlogInvitationCreated, ChangedPublication, EmailChanged, GameInactivityWarning, NewBlogComment, NewGlobalChatMessage, NewPublication, PasswordChanged, RoomPendencyFulfilled, RoomPendencyReminder, TicketCreated, WarningIssued. Of those, 11 are actively produced by domain code: BlogInvitationService, BlogService (ChangedPublication, NewPublication), EmailChangeService, GameInactivityProcessor, BlogCommentService + PublicationCommentService (NewBlogComment), MessageService.cs:137 (NewGlobalChatMessage), PasswordChangeService, PostService (RoomPendencyFulfilled), PendencyReminderService (RoomPendencyReminder). `dm.

**Последствие.** Users receive no notification for new publications, new blog comments, blog invitations, password changes, email changes, game inactivity warnings, or pendency reminders/fulfilments. The security-relevant ones (PasswordChanged, EmailChanged) are exactly the notifications a user needs to detect an account takeover. Global chat realtime push is entirely dead — the client silently falls back to 30-second polling, which is why the fallback poller looks like it works. Nothing logs, nothing alerts; the pipeline reports 100% success while a third of it is disconnected.

**Исправление.** Stop hand-maintaining the routing key list. Derive the bindings from the registered `INotificationGenerator` set at startup — resolve `IEnumerable<INotificationGenerator>` before building the consumer and bind the union of their `CanResolve` types. Add a startup assertion (or an xunit test over the `EventType` enum) that every produced event type is bound by at least one consumer, and configure an alternate-exchange on `dm.events` so unroutable publishes land somewhere visible instead of vanishing.

**Проверка оппонентом.** The routing gap is real and I could not refute it. D:\Code Projects\dm3\src\DM.Workers.NotificationDispatcher\NotificationDispatcherConsumer.cs:38-108 binds `dm.notifications` to `dm.events` with 41 literal routing keys (D:\Code Projects\dm3\src\DM.Infrastructure.Core\Extensions\InvokedEventTypeExtensions.cs:18-20 resolves each EventType to its [EventRoutingKey] string — no wildcards anywhere). The exchange really is a topic exchange with no catch-all: the Jamq.Client.Rabbit 0.10.0 assembly contains exactly one exchange-type string literal, "topic", and zero occurrences of "alternate-exchange"/"x-dead-letter", so unroutable publishes are dropped silently. No other binding compensates — the only other consumers are SearchIndexerConsumer.cs:54 (its own curated key list), MailConsumer.cs:46-50 (`#` but on a different exchange), and Realtime/RealtimeNotificationConsumer.cs:35-40 (`#` on the 

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] MOJIBAKE-NOTIFICATIONS — 109 Cyrillic string literals in the email and bot senders are destroyed — notification emails ship with subject "???? ????????????"

**Статус: Исправлено.** То же, что HYG-03 (304e618a)

**Доказательство.** `NotificationEmailSender.cs:27-...` (`EventTypeSubjects`) contains 55 literals of the form `[EventType.StatusGameActive] = "???? ????????????"`, and `NotificationBotSender.cs:30-...` (`EventTypeMessages`) contains 54 of the same. The damage is partial, which proves it is encoding corruption rather than placeholders: within the same dictionary, `[EventType.StatusBlogActive] = "Блог открыт"` and the subscription entries are intact while everything above them is destroyed. The files have no UTF-8 BOM (first bytes are `using`), so an ANSI round-trip flattened the pre-existing Cyrillic to `?`. These literals feed the live paths at NotificationEmailSender.cs:161-163 (`$"Dungeon Master: {subj}"`) and NotificationEmailSender.cs:196 (the `<h2>` in the HTML body). Two further files are affected: BlogInvitationNotificationGenerator.cs:49 (`? "?????????" : "?????????"` — both branches now identical)

**Последствие.** Every notification email for ~55 event types goes out with a subject line and body heading of literal question marks, as does every Discord/Telegram push. This is the most visible artifact of the entire async pipeline and it is broken for the majority of event types. BlogInvitationNotificationGenerator.cs:49 is additionally a logic defect: the ternary that distinguishes assistant from reader now returns the same string on both branches.

**Исправление.** Restore the strings from git history (`git show <pre-corruption-rev>:<path>`) rather than retyping, re-save all four files as UTF-8 with BOM, and add `*.cs text working-tree-encoding=UTF-8` plus a CI grep for `"[^"]*\?{3,}[^"]*"` in .cs files so this cannot recur silently.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Verified at byte level, and the refutation attempt failed on every ground. (1) Not a rendering artifact: NotificationEmailSender.cs:30 contains literal 0x3f bytes while line 65 in the SAME dictionary contains valid UTF-8 Cyrillic (d0 91 d0 bb d0 be d0 b3 = "Блог"); the file has no BOM (first bytes 75 73 69 6e 67 = "using"). Same proof pattern in BbParserProvider.cs, where the corrupted Nsfw tag (line 30) and Private tags (lines 75, 83) sit two lines from an intact Spoiler tag (lines 50-51, "Скрыть содержимое"/"Показать содержимое"). (2) Not dead code: NotificationProcessor.cs:79 and :86 call _emailSender.SendIfEnabled / _botSender.SendIfEnabled unconditionally for every generated notification, both registered at Startup.cs:109,114; NotificationCategoryMapper.GetCategory maps Games/Forum/Blog/Messages/Security/Moderation (lines 16-57), so the corrupted event types never hit the `_ => null

### [ВЫСОКАЯ] DEAD-DLX — The mail worker's dead-letter exchange is declared but never bound to anything — there is no poison-message handling in the system

**Статус: Исправлено.** Рабочая очередь привязана к DLX, DLQ сделана терминальной вместо петли с TTL; топология проверена в живом брокере (5f86697c)

**Доказательство.** `MailConsumer.ConfigureDLX()` (MailConsumer.cs:58-73) opens a dedicated connection, declares the `dm.mail.unsent` fanout exchange and a `dm.mail.unsent-dlq` queue with `x-dead-letter-exchange: dm.mail.sending` and `x-message-ttl: 60000`, then binds the DLQ to the DLX. But `RabbitConsumerParameters` exposes a settable `DeadLetterExchange` property (confirmed in the Jamq.Client.Rabbit 0.10.0 metadata: `get_DeadLetterExchange`/`set_DeadLetterExchange`), and a repo-wide grep for `DeadLetterExchange` finds only MailConsumer's own private const — the consumer queue at MailConsumer.cs:46-50 is declared without it. Nothing ever publishes to `dm.mail.unsent`. Separately, `ProcessResult` has `Failure` and `RetryNeeded` members (Jamq.Client.Abstractions), and a grep for `ProcessResult.` across src/ returns five hits, all `.Success` — the application has no code path that signals failure to the brok

**Последствие.** A permanently-failing message (malformed address, oversized attachment, a generator that throws on a deleted entity) has nowhere to go. It exhausts the five in-process Polly retries, the exception escapes, and its fate is decided entirely by the library default rather than by anything this codebase chose — either an infinite redelivery loop that pins a consumer, or a silent drop. Neither is observable. The DLX code creates a convincing appearance of poison-message handling in code review and in the RabbitMQ management UI, which is worse than having none. Note also that even if it were wired, the 60s-TTL-back-to-the-source design is an infinite retry loop with no attempt ceiling.

**Исправление.** Set `DeadLetterExchange = DeadLetterExchangeName` on the `RabbitConsumerParameters` at MailConsumer.cs:46, and do the same for the notification and search consumers (which declare no DLX at all). Replace the TTL-loop-back topology with a terminal DLQ plus an `x-delivery-limit` (quorum queue) or a retry counter in headers, and have the processors return `ProcessResult.Failure` for non-retryable errors instead of throwing.

**Проверка оппонентом.** Every verifiable claim holds; only the impact narrative needs correcting (and the real failure mode is worse than the one described).

VERIFIED AS STATED:
1. D:\Code Projects\dm3\src\DM.Workers.Mail\MailConsumer.cs:58-73 — ConfigureDLX() opens a dedicated connection, declares fanout `dm.mail.unsent` (line 66), declares `dm.mail.unsent-dlq` with `{"x-dead-letter-exchange","dm.mail.sending"}` + `{"x-message-ttl",60000}` (lines 67-71), binds DLQ->DLX (line 72). Exactly as the finding describes.
2. MailConsumer.cs:46-50 — `new RabbitConsumerParameters("dm.mail.sender","dm.mail.sending",ProcessingOrder.Sequential){ ExchangeName=..., RoutingKeys=... }`. `DeadLetterExchange` is NOT set.
3. The property is real and is the only wiring mechanism: I dumped the #US/strings heap of C:\Users\Ivan\.nuget\packages\jamq.client.rabbit\0.10.0\lib\net6.0\Jamq.Client.Rabbit.dll and found `get_DeadLetterExcha
